# FE Element, Shadow DOM, Svelte integration, editing core and views

This epic turns [02 Frontend](../spec/02-frontend.md) into stories: the `<doc-editor>` element and its lifecycle, packaging and lazy chunks, the shadow root (styles, Trusted Types, theming mechanism, overlays, keyboard scoping, composed events), the Svelte integration, the document API, the building blocks of the editing core (02 §5.2), the recovery journal, fonts on the client, the web view, the read view, the page view pipeline, math display and editing in the editor, accessibility of the editing surface, internationalisation, browser support and the frontend test matrices. Sources: 02; ADR 0002, 0003 and 0005; the review's section 3.3 (findings F1 to F15) and section 6; phase placement from [11 Roadmap](../spec/11-roadmap.md), 12 §13 and 13 §12. Every number is a target to validate.

Phase 3 stories (the page view, 02 §9) are gated on the evidence review at the end of phase 2 (story SPK-013). They are written against injected interfaces (a text-measurement provider, a break-opportunity provider, a line breaker and the layout engine API) and stay language-neutral: any Rust/WebAssembly implementation of measurement, shaping or line breaking belongs to the WASM epic and plugs in behind those interfaces.

What this epic leaves to other epics (README §2.3):

- **PRO** owns the embedding contract (attributes, properties, methods, events; 05 §8). FE writes the element internals that make it true.
- **SYN** owns the sync client algorithm (04 §8) and comment-range mapping in the authority. FE writes its integration into the editing core.
- **PRM** owns what a person sees when permissions block or reject an edit (07 §7). FE writes the guard mechanics.
- **CDM** owns the model, the schema generation and the shared numbering and formatting algorithms. **EXP** owns the math gate, LaTeX, `layout.json` and the export job. **BE** owns the font manifest and assets.
- **UX** owns the shell, the command registry, panes, the save-state indicator, the recovered-edits pane, review tools, presence drawing, the dark and high-contrast themes, replaceable icons and strings, and the accessibility checker. **FRW** owns field nodes, field node views and fill mode (12 §11). **SPK** owns spikes S1, S2, S5, S7 and S9. **FND** owns the performance harness and CI gates; FE states the budget criteria for its own work.

## Packaging and registration (02 §2)

### FE-001 Register the element safely

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1a | M | must | Integrating developer |

**Spec:** 02 §2; ADR 0002 · **Requirements:** R2, R12 · **Depends on:** PRO-076

As an integrating developer, I want importing the module to register `doc-editor` without surprises, so that the editor works on any page, on a server and next to another version of itself.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the module is imported in a runtime with no DOM (Node.js, server-side rendering), when the import runs, then it does not throw, defines no element and reads no browser global.
- [x] Given a browser page where `doc-editor` is not defined, when the module is imported, then `customElements.get("doc-editor")` returns the exported class.
- [x] Given `doc-editor` is already defined by a different version of this class, when the module is imported, then it logs one clear warning that names both versions, leaves the existing definition in place and does not bind the host silently to the older class.
- [x] Given `doc-editor` is already defined by the same version, when the module is imported, then it neither warns nor throws.
- [x] Given the exported class, when a host registers it under another tag name or in a scoped custom element registry, then the element mounts and works exactly as under the default name.
- [x] Given the class source, when it is inspected, then it extends `HTMLElement` directly and is not produced by Svelte's `customElement` compiler option (ADR 0002).

**Evidence:** packages/element/test/register.test.ts (the entry imported by Node.js without a DOM: no throw, no definition, existence checks only, no browser global read; defineDocEditor: same version silent, another version one warning naming both, a subclass for a second name); e2e/element.spec.ts 'FE-001 registration' in Chromium, Edge, Firefox and WebKit (the library build registers its exported class; another version warns once and stays; the same version is silent; another tag name mounts and edits everywhere, a scoped registry where the browser has one: Chromium, Edge and Firefox, WebKit 27.2 has none and skips that part); test/source.test.ts (no Svelte customElement option; the class extends HTMLElement); src/register.ts, src/index.ts, src/doc-editor.ts; ADR 0039 §1 (proposed). Run of 2026-11-06, 17:39 UTC, the whole element suite in four browsers: 780 passed, 0 failed, 1 flaky (passed on retry), 455 skipped by design.

**Notes:** Review F6 (server import, silent version collisions). How the module recognises "a different version of this class" is not specified; the static `version` property of 05 §8 is the obvious carrier. Packet B2 (2026-11-06): the element compares `version` (the embedding API) and `release` (the package version) together (ADR 0039 §1, proposed).

### FE-002 Build one ES module with lazy chunks

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1a | M | must | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 02 §2; 13 §9; ADR 0002 · **Requirements:** R2 · **Depends on:** FND-002, FND-008

As a maintainer, I want one ECMAScript module with lazily loaded chunks, so that the first interactive editor stays small and heavy parts load only when someone uses them.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given a production build, when it finishes, then Vite library mode has produced one ES module entry, separate lazy chunks for the PDF viewer, its worker, the math editor and each pane, and the component CSS as a separate stylesheet file.
- [x] Given a document opened in the web view with no pane opened and no equation present, when the network requests are listed, then none of the lazy chunks was requested.
- [x] Given the initial chunk, when CI measures it, then its compressed size is at most 320 KB (target; owner decision 32, it was 300 KB) and the build fails when the budget is exceeded.
- [x] Given the contents of the shadow root, when they are inspected, then no custom element defined by this project is nested inside the editor; inner pieces are ordinary Svelte components.
- [ ] Given a third-party piece that registers global custom elements (the math editor), when the editor starts, then none of its tag names is registered, and when the piece loads on demand, then exactly the tag names listed in the integration guide appear.
- [x] Given the published package, when a host bundler imports it, then the lazy chunks resolve from the module's own location, so that lazy loading works without host configuration.

**Evidence:** packages/element/e2e/element.spec.ts 'FE-002 lazy chunks of the library build' in Chromium, Edge, Firefox and WebKit (pages/lib.html loads dist/lib/doc-editor.js by URL: opening a document requests the entry, the editor and engine chunks and their imports only, all from the module's own directory, no pane, viewer or DEP client; a pane loads when opened; no custom element of the project inside the root); scripts/build.ts and scripts/bundle-report.ts (the package's build fails when the first interactive editor is over 300,000 bytes of gzip; measured 2026-11-06 with Node.js zlib, gzip level 9, on the minified library build: 157.4 kB with a host backend, 209.7 kB with the DEP client; dist/lib/bundle-report.json); ADR 0039 §2. Run of 2026-11-06, 17:39 UTC, the whole element suite in four browsers: 780 passed, 0 failed, 1 flaky (passed on retry), 455 skipped by design.

**Notes:** 02 §2 lists the PDF viewer, its worker, the math editor and the panes as lazy. It does not say whether KaTeX display (FE-069) is lazy or in the initial chunk (see Open questions). The last criterion is a consequence of "lazily loaded chunks" in a distributable module, not a stated requirement. Packet B2 (2026-11-06): criteria 1 and 5 stay open. The math editor (FE-070) is not in the product build yet, so it has no chunk and its tag names cannot be checked there (spike S2's page still loads MathLive on demand); and the CSS reaches the shadow root as a string in the editor chunk, the path spike S2 verified under a strict policy, so the build emits no separate stylesheet file (ADR 0039 §3, proposed). "The initial chunk" is measured as the first interactive editor: the entry, the editor chunk and their static imports (ADR 0039 §2). Lane V-FE-001-016 (2026-11-14), a build run with `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts` (the web server builds the library): criterion 1 stays open for two reasons. A separate stylesheet file would conflict with FE-014 and FE-015, which adopt the CSS as a string into a constructable sheet and a nonce `<style>`; the spec default is a file, so the choice is for the owner or a proposed ADR. The math editor chunk (FE-070) is still absent. Criterion 5 stays open because FE-070 is not built. The budget check in the same build reports 242.4 kB gzip for the host-backend first interactive editor and 299.4 kB with the DEP client, both within 300.0 kB. Lane w1 (2026-11-15), nothing new ticked; checked against the library build in `packages/element/dist/lib` (bundle report of 2026-11-15). The build has one entry (`doc-editor.js`), a chunk for each pane, the PDF viewer frame with its own page and worker under `viewer/`, and no MathLive chunk: `src/features/math.ts` (`openMathEditor`) is imported only by `src/testing.ts`, so the product build never reaches it. Criteria 1 and 5 therefore still wait for FE-070 to wire the math editor into the editor; making a chunk for it here would be scaffolding for a story that is not built. Criterion 1 also still needs the owner's word on the stylesheet: 02 §2 says an external stylesheet file, ADR 0039 §3 (proposed) ships the CSS as a string in the editor chunk so that a strict policy needs no `style-src` entry (FE-014, FE-015). No change was made. Lane w2 (2026-11-18): the budget in criterion 3 reads 320 KB (owner decision 32). The gate works as written (the build fails above it) but the build is over it, 359.6 kB gzip with the host backend and 404.3 kB with the DEP client; the size work and what remains are in FND-008 Notes. The end-to-end suites could not be run in this worktree: the demo build needs the layout kernel's files, which need Podman (`pnpm --filter @docedit/layout-kernel build:wasm`), and they are not built here. The lazy shell parts are covered by the Svelte compile and the type check only.

## Element lifecycle (02 §2.1)

### FE-003 Start loading when the host has set everything, in any order

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1a | M | must | Integrating developer |

**Spec:** 02 §2.1; ADR 0002 (after the first review) · **Requirements:** R2, R6 · **Depends on:** FE-001, PRO-074, PRO-075

As an integrating developer, I want the element to begin loading only when a document identifier and a token source are both present, whatever order my framework assigns them in, so that I need not know how the framework sets properties.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a framework assigns `getToken` before the class is defined, when the element upgrades, then the constructor captures the value and the editor uses it; it is neither lost nor left shadowing the class's accessor.
- [x] Given `doc-id` and `getToken` set in either order, or `doc-id` and `backend` set in either order, when the second one arrives, then loading starts exactly once, in a microtask, in every order.
- [x] Given only one of the two is present, when the element is connected, then no request is made and no error is raised.
- [x] Given the element is constructed, when the constructor returns, then the shadow root is attached and properties set before upgrade (`backend`, `config`, `searchPrincipals`, `resolveUsers`, `extensions`) are captured as well.
- [x] Given a mounted element, when an observed attribute changes, then `attributeChangedCallback` reflects it into the application without remounting it; changing `doc-id` loads the other document.

**Evidence:** packages/element/e2e/element.spec.ts 'FE-003 loading when everything is set, in any order' in Chromium, Edge, Firefox and WebKit (properties set before the class is defined, getToken, backend, config, searchPrincipals, resolveUsers and extensions, are captured without shadowing the accessors; nine orders of doc-id, backend or getToken and connecting: nothing in the task that sets them, then exactly one load and one de-ready; one of the two alone: no request, no event; toolbar, locale, theme and mode changes keep the shell and the core, and the shell turns right-to-left for ar-XB; doc-id loads the other document); src/doc-editor.ts (constructor, #reconcile), src/session.ts. Run of 2026-11-06, 17:39 UTC, the whole element suite in four browsers: 780 passed, 0 failed, 1 flaky (passed on retry), 455 skipped by design.

**Notes:** Review F6. The list of attributes and properties and their meaning is PRO's (05 §8.1); this story is the mechanism.

### FE-004 Mount the application once and resume it on later connects

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1a | M | must | Integrating developer |

**Spec:** 02 §2.1, §4; ADR 0002 · **Requirements:** R2 · **Depends on:** FE-003, FE-013

As an integrating developer, I want the element to mount its Svelte application the first time it connects and to resume it on later connects, so that editor state survives whatever my framework does with the element.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the element connects for the first time, when `connectedCallback` runs, then the application is mounted with `mount(App, { target: shadowRoot, props })` and the editing core is created exactly once.
- [x] Given the element has been connected before, when it connects again, then no second application and no second core are created, global listeners are re-attached and the editor resumes.
- [x] Given a mounted editor, when `unmount` is called, then everything the application rendered is removed from the shadow root.
- [x] Given two instances on one page, when both are mounted, then each has its own core and state, and they share only the constructable stylesheet (FE-014) and font registrations (FE-061).
- [x] Given a host that itself uses Svelte 5, when both applications run on one page, then neither affects the other's state or context.

**Evidence:** packages/element/e2e/fe-004-reconnect.spec.ts 'FE-004 criterion 2' (lane V-FE-001-016, run 2026-11-14 with `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts` on demo port 57001, Chromium, Firefox and WebKit: 6 passed; two same-task moves keep one editing surface with the same node, raise no second de-ready, and typing afterwards reaches the document; a press outside after a move clears focus and a later move does not restore it, so the document pointerdown listener is re-attached on each connect), packages/element/e2e/element.spec.ts 'FE-004 mount once, resume on later connects' in Chromium, Edge, Firefox and WebKit (one shell and one core; a move in one task keeps both and raises no de-ready; destroy() leaves the shadow root empty; two instances have their own cores and share one adopted sheet) and s1-shadow-root.spec.ts (lifecycle); s2-svelte-host.spec.ts (a host with its own Svelte 5 keeps working next to the editor); src/editor.ts (mount(App, { target: shadowRoot, props }), unmount). Run of 2026-11-06, 17:39 UTC, the whole element suite in four browsers: 780 passed, 0 failed, 1 flaky (passed on retry), 455 skipped by design.

**Notes:** The Svelte `customElement` option is rejected because it destroys the inner component a microtask after disconnect (ADR 0002); this story is the positive form of that decision. Packet B2 (2026-11-06): criterion 2 stays open for its listener half. Lane V-FE-001-016 (2026-11-14) closed it with fe-004-reconnect.spec.ts (see Evidence). Before that, a reconnect created no second application or core (tested), but in phase 1a the element puts no listener of its own on `document` or `window` (the PDF viewer talks over a `MessagePort`, the shortcut listener sits on the shadow root), and removing listeners on suspend, ProseMirror's included, is FE-005.

### FE-005 Survive being moved in the DOM

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Integrating developer |

**Spec:** 02 §2.1; ADR 0002 (after the first review) · **Requirements:** R2 · **Depends on:** FE-004

As an integrating developer, I want the element to suspend and resume instead of being destroyed when a framework moves it, so that no editor state is thrown away.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a host removes the element and inserts it again in the same task, when `disconnectedCallback` has waited one microtask, then the element is connected again, nothing was suspended, and the selection, scroll position and unconfirmed work are unchanged.
- [x] Given a browser that can move an element without disconnecting it, when the host moves it that way, then `connectedMoveCallback` does nothing.
- [ ] Given the element is still disconnected after that microtask, when it suspends, then it removes its listeners on `document` and `window`, stops observers and workers and closes the socket, and keeps its editing core and journal.
- [x] Given a suspended element reconnects within the grace period, when `connectedCallback` runs, then it re-attaches listeners, reopens the channel with a resume (04 §9) and continues with local edits intact.
- [x] Given a framework list that reorders the element 50 times, when the reorders finish, then the editing core instance is the same one that existed before (identity preserved).

**Evidence:** packages/element/e2e/fe-lifecycle.spec.ts 'FE-005 survive being moved in the DOM' in Chromium, 2026-11-13 (a move to the other container in one task keeps the core, leaves the element unsuspended with no grace timer and keeps the unconfirmed typing in the body; a native `moveBefore` keeps the element connected with no disconnectedCallback or connectedCallback call, where the browser has `Element.moveBefore`; fifty moves in separate tasks keep one core and one shell and never suspend the element). Firefox and WebKit were not run for this file in this round. Lane V-FE-001-016 (2026-11-13), criterion 1: packages/element/e2e/fe-005-move-selection.spec.ts 'selection and scroll position survive remove and insert in one task' (Playwright, Chromium, `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=57002 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/fe-005-move-selection.spec.ts --project=chromium`, passed without retry: the caret after a click and typing, and the page scroll position at 120, are the same after a remove and insert in one task); the same run of packages/element/e2e/fe-lifecycle.spec.ts, element.spec.ts (FE-003, FE-004, FE-013, PRO-076 tests) and fe-005-move-selection.spec.ts: 22 passed, Chromium. Lane w1 (2026-11-15), criteria 4 and 5, and the tested parts of 3: `packages/engine/test/sync-resume.test.ts` 'suspend and resume' (the sync session closes its channel and opens none while suspended; a resume reopens with the confirmed seq and the in-flight commit and sends what waited); `packages/element/test/fe-005-session-suspend.test.ts` (the session removes and restores its `visibilitychange` listener, suspends and resumes the editor, closes and reconnects a backend it created, leaves a host's backend alone, keeps the editor when the reconnect fails); `packages/element/e2e/fe-005-suspend-resume.spec.ts` (real core and shell on the embed page: during a suspension the element's `pointerdown` and `visibilitychange` listeners, its `ResizeObserver` and its colour-scheme listener are off and the core is the same and offline; after reconnecting they are back, an edit made while suspended is still in the editor and reaches a second editor, and typing goes on) and `packages/element/e2e/fe-lifecycle.spec.ts` (fifty moves keep one core and one shell), run with `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=57102 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts` over those two files and `fe-005-move-selection.spec.ts`, projects chromium, msedge, firefox and webkit, `--retries=0`, on the lane's Windows 11 machine: 28 passed, 4 skipped by the files (the move-selection test outside Chromium; native `moveBefore` in WebKit). With a real socket: the third test of `tests/V-FE-113-128/fe-122-two-tabs.spec.ts` (element with `endpoint` against `services/document-service` on PostgreSQL in the lane's Podman container `docedit-pg-w1`): the suspended element closes its WebSocket, the reconnected one opens a second, receives the change it missed and its typing reaches the other tab; `pnpm exec playwright test -c tests/V-FE-113-128/playwright.config.ts --project=<browser>`, no retry: passed in Chromium (2 of 3 runs of the file; in the first run an older test of the file, 'two tabs on one service', failed at its last step and the rest did not run), Firefox (1 run) and WebKit (1 run). After the lane's fix commit (the shell's colour-scheme watcher is stopped once per suspension; `suspendSync` and `resumeSync` named in `packages/engine/test/api.test.ts`), `fe-005-suspend-resume.spec.ts` was run again in the four browsers: 8 passed.

**Notes:** Lane w1 (2026-11-15): criteria 4 and 5 ticked; criterion 3 stays open. Built: `SyncSession.suspend` and `resume` (engine), `Session.suspend` and `resume` (element), `setSuspended` of the shell (`packages/ui/src/shell/suspension.ts`). Criterion 5 is tested with DOM moves, which is what a keyed list of a framework does; no framework runtime is in the test. Why criterion 3 is not ticked: (a) measured in `fe-005-suspend-resume.spec.ts`, two kinds of listener stay on `document` during a suspension: ProseMirror's `selectionchange` listener (one per view, removed only when the view is destroyed) and Svelte's delegated-event handler (click, focusin, input, keydown, mousedown and pointermove in this build, removed only at `unmount`). Neither library has a public way to take them off and put them back; destroying the views and the shell on suspension would throw away the shell's state, which the story forbids. Open question for the owner: accept these two (and reword the criterion) or require an unmount on suspension. (b) Workers: the element starts none itself during a session; a PDF viewer's frame, with its PDF.js worker, is unloaded by the browser when the element leaves the document, which was not measured. (c) Socket: a backend the element created from `endpoint` is closed and connected again; a backend the host passed keeps its socket, because the host owns it and may share it; only the channel is closed. That reading is the lane's, not the spec's. A session that is still loading when the element leaves finishes its load and is suspended then. One flaky failure seen under load in the older two-tab test (text 'ammaBeta' for 'Gamma'): a typed replacement that raced a remote undo; not investigated. Earlier: Review F6 (lifecycle leaks). Packet D1 (2026-11-13): criterion 1 stays open: the test checks the core, the suspension and the typed text, but not the selection or the scroll position after a move. Criteria 3 and 4 stay open: suspending does not yet remove listeners, close the channel or reopen it with a resume. Criteria 2 and 5 are verified in Chromium only. Lane V-FE-001-016 (2026-11-13): criterion 1 is ticked on e2e/fe-005-move-selection.spec.ts (the test was `fixme`; it is now a real test). Cause: the browser blurs the focused editor during the removal, while the element is still connected, and drops the selection with the subtree. The element now records focus (`#focusWithin`, cleared by focus moving to another element or by a press outside it) and puts focus back on connect when the editor had it (src/doc-editor.ts). The scroll position is the page's, not the editor's: the editor has no scroll container of its own (shell.css), and a same-task move keeps the page scroll. Criteria 3 and 4 stay open (suspending and reconnecting with a resume, as before). Decision recorded in docs/adr/0300-lane-V-FE-001-016.md. Lane U-FE-001-016 (2026-11-13): criterion 1 is tested in e2e/fe-005-move-selection.spec.ts and fails: a same-task remove and insert leaves the shadow root with no selection and resets the scroll position (Chromium: selection empty after the move, scroll 0 against 17 before). The element has no step that restores them, so criterion 1 stays open; the test is `fixme` until src/doc-editor.ts restores them. Lane FE-001-013 (2026-11-13): the session has no suspend or resume step yet. `disconnectedCallback` only sets the suspended flag and the grace timer (src/doc-editor.ts), so the channel stays open while suspended and no resume with `resume` is sent on reconnect; criterion 4 needs that step and the core's re-attach, which the engine owns. The element puts no listener on `document` or `window` (grep of src/ finds only the PDF viewer frame's own listeners), so criterion 2's listener half has nothing to remove yet.

### FE-006 Destroy the editor completely

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Integrating developer |

**Spec:** 02 §2.1; ADR 0002 (after the first review) · **Requirements:** R2, R15 · **Depends on:** FE-005, FE-008

As an integrating developer, I want a removed editor to destroy itself after a grace period, so that single-page applications do not leak editors.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the element stays disconnected for the grace period (default 30 seconds) and has no `keep-alive` attribute, when the period ends, then it destroys itself.
- [x] Given the `keep-alive` attribute is present, when the element stays disconnected for longer than the grace period, then it remains suspended and is not destroyed.
- [x] Given `destroy()` is called or the grace period ends, when destruction completes, then Svelte is unmounted, the editing core is destroyed, and every worker, channel, timer, observer, listener and open IndexedDB handle is released, verified by instrumented counters and without relying on garbage collection.
- [x] Given unconfirmed commits exist when the editor is destroyed, when it is destroyed, then they remain in the journal and are resubmitted on the next load (FE-009).
- [x] Given a single-page application mounts and removes a route containing the editor 100 times, when memory is measured after forced garbage collection, then no editor instance is retained.

**Evidence:** packages/element/e2e/fe-006-memory.spec.ts 'FE-006 memory after repeated mount and removal' in Chromium (lane U-FE-001-016, 2026-11-13: 100 mount and remove cycles, each left 31 seconds on Playwright's fake clock so the grace period ends; `HeapProfiler.collectGarbage` twice; a WeakRef to each element and each editing core, 200 in all: some still resolve before the collection, none after it; run alone, 1.6 minutes, passed); packages/element/e2e/fe-lifecycle.spec.ts 'FE-006 destroy after the grace period' in Chromium, 2026-11-13, with Playwright's fake clock (a removed editor is still suspended with its shell at 27 seconds and has no session after 30.5 seconds, so the default is 30 seconds; with `keep-alive`, the editor stays suspended and mounted after 60 seconds, and connecting it again resumes it with one shell).

**Evidence (lane w1, 2026-11-17, criterion 3):** `packages/element/e2e/fe-006-release.spec.ts` with the counters in `packages/element/e2e/fe-006-counters.ts`. The counters wrap the page's own timers, animation frames, event targets, observers, workers, IndexedDB, WebSocket and BroadcastChannel; a baseline is read before the editor mounts and compared after it is destroyed, with no garbage collection and with Playwright's fake clock not advanced after an explicit `destroy()`. Cases: 'destroy() unmounts the shell and the core and releases every resource it took' (the shell's `.de-shell` is gone and the editor handle is null; the editor took 11 listeners on the window, document and other long-lived targets, 2 observers and 1 open IndexedDB handle, `docedit-journal`, and after the destroy none is held), 'the end of the grace period releases the same, with the element removed' (31 seconds of fake clock), and a control, 'a listener, an interval, an observer and an open database show up, and go when released', which shows that the counters see each of those four kinds. Run on 2026-11-17 on the development machine with `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts fe-006-release --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit --retries=0`: 9 passed. The channel and the backend closing are counted at the session level in `packages/element/test/fe-006-release.test.ts`.

**Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-17):** Criterion 3 is ticked with this scope: on the embed page the backend is in memory and the editor creates no worker and no WebSocket, so those two counters read no acquisition (the PDF viewer's worker lives in the viewer's own frame, not in this page). They are installed and would show one; they have not been shown to release one. Only listeners on targets that outlive the editor are counted; those on nodes inside its own tree go with the tree. Criterion 4 was left to FE-009 and is ticked with it: `packages/element/e2e/fe-009-resubmit-reopen.spec.ts` 'FE-009 criterion 1' calls `destroy()` on an element whose commit never reached the service (the commit stays in IndexedDB), then mounts a new element on the same document, which sends the commit again; the journal empties and the text is in the service's document once (Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 2026-11-17, with the FE-009 run).

**Notes:** Review F6. How the grace period is configured, and what happens if a destroyed element is connected again, are not specified (Open questions). Packet D1 (2026-11-13): criteria 3 and 5 stay open: there are no instrumented release counters, and the 100-route memory check is not written. Lane U-FE-001-016 (2026-11-13): criterion 5 is ticked on e2e/fe-006-memory.spec.ts (Chromium only: the forced collection uses the DevTools Protocol). Criterion 3 stays open: the grace timer, workers, observers, shell listeners and IndexedDB handles have no release counters in the browser yet. Lane FE-001-013 (2026-11-13): session-level counters are in packages/element/test/fe-006-release.test.ts (one channel close or more, one detach, one editor destroy, one backend close, subscriptions back to zero, nothing reported after close; 1 test passing in Node.js). Criterion 3 stays open: the grace timer, workers, observers, shell listeners and IndexedDB handles are not counted in Node, and the browser counters are not written. Criterion 4 stays open until the journal (FE-008) exists. The two ticked criteria are verified in Chromium only.

## Not losing work: the recovery journal (02 §2.2)

### FE-007 Expose the save state

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1a | S | must | Author |

**Spec:** 02 §2.2; 13 §3 · **Requirements:** R15 · **Depends on:** FE-033, PRO-087, UX-014, UX-016

As an author, I want the editor to know whether my work is saved, saving or read-only at all times, so that the header and the host can show it truthfully.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given nothing is queued or in flight, when the sync client is idle, then the save state is `saved`.
- [x] Given local steps are queued or a commit is in flight, when the state is read, then it is `saving`; when the commit is acknowledged, then it returns to `saved`.
- [x] Given the user's effective rights allow no editing, or the server answers `read_only`, when the state is read, then it is `readonly`.
- [x] Given the connection changes between online, reconnecting and offline, when it changes, then the connection value follows it, independently of the sync value.
- [x] Given any change of the save state, when it happens, then the core's read-only state view updates and `de-status` is dispatched with `connection` and `sync` (composed, FE-022), at most once per animation frame.

**Evidence:** packages/element/test/save-state.test.ts 'FE-007 criterion 3' (the session on backend-mock with the real headless core: the document is locked while a commit is held, the room refuses it with `read_only`, and the save state reads `readonly` through the session and the core; `saved` before), with the rights case in packages/element/test/session.test.ts 'reports readonly when the access allows no change (FE-007)'; packages/element/test/session.test.ts 'follows the DEP socket connection, independently of the save state (FE-007)' (round 1: a DEP-style socket moves online, reconnecting, offline and online while the save state moves saving and saved, one de-status per frame with the connection and the save state read separately); packages/element/test/status.test.ts and test/session.test.ts (at most one de-status per frame, only on change; saved, saving, saved from the core's save state; readonly when the access allows no change); e2e/element.spec.ts 'FE-007 save state' in Chromium, Edge, Firefox and WebKit (saving in the task of a local change, saved after the acknowledgement, readonly for a viewer, offline after a revocation while the save state stays; de-status composed and bubbling); src/status.ts, src/editor.ts (status view), src/session.ts. Run of 2026-11-06, 17:39 UTC, the whole element suite in four browsers: 780 passed, 0 failed, 1 flaky (passed on retry), 455 skipped by design.

**Notes:** 1a shows the header with presence and save state (11 §3). The fourth state, `unsaved` (in the journal only), arrives with FE-008. Packet B2 (2026-11-06): criteria 3 and 4 stay open. `readonly` from an access that allows no change is tested; `readonly` after a `read_only` rejection is the core's (FE-029). `online` and `offline` are tested through the element; `reconnecting` needs a transport that drops, which the DEP client's own tests drive (PRO-093), not yet the element's. Round 1 (element packet): the session test above drives the DEP socket's `reconnecting` and `offline` values through the element's session, so criterion 4 is ticked on that evidence. Round 8 (element packet, 2026-11-13): criterion 3 is ticked (save-state.test.ts sends the `read_only` answer from the mock room; see ADR 0109, round 8).

### FE-008 Write unconfirmed commits to the journal before sending them

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | L | must | Author |

**Spec:** 02 §2.2; 04 §8, §2 (G4); 13 §9; 10 §3.1 · **Requirements:** R15 · **Depends on:** FE-033, FE-007, SYN-017, SYN-018

As an author, I want my unsent and unconfirmed work written to the browser's IndexedDB before it is sent, so that a closed or crashed tab does not lose it.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the sync client is about to send a commit, when it sends, then the commit has first been written to the journal keyed by its commit identifier, and the send happens only after that write has resolved.
- [x] Given local steps are queued but not yet sent, when the queue changes, then the journal holds the in-flight commit, the queue and any recovered edits (04 §8).
- [x] Given the server acknowledges a commit, when the acknowledgement is processed, then the commit's journal entry is removed.
- [x] Given a commit is in the journal and unconfirmed while the connection is down or the service cannot acknowledge (06: database unavailable), when the state is read, then it is `unsaved`; when the commit is confirmed, then it is `saved`.
- [x] Given the journal is inspected after a session, when its contents are read, then it holds commits and never tokens (10 §3.1).
- [x] Given the tab is killed between the journal write and the send, when the document is opened again, then the entry is still present.

**Evidence:** criterion 6 (round 3 fix, lane U-FE-001-016, 2026-11-13): packages/element/e2e/fe-008-journal-reopen.spec.ts (Playwright, `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=56003 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/fe-008-journal-reopen.spec.ts`, 4 passed of 4 in Chromium, Edge, Firefox and WebKit, run twice on 2026-11-13 on the shared development machine): Ada types in `<doc-editor>` with its default IndexedDB journal, the channel's `submit` is blocked so the send never reaches the service, the test waits until the commit is in IndexedDB, then kills the tab (Chromium and Edge: renderer crash through CDP `Page.crash`; Firefox and WebKit: page closed without `beforeunload`, as they offer no crash), opens the page in a new tab of the same browser context, opens the same document in a new `<doc-editor>` and reads IndexedDB: the commit is there under the same identifier, and with the journalled queue it holds every typed character. Commits refused or set aside leave the journal (round 3 fix, packages/engine/test/fe-008-journal-reject.test.ts, Node.js, 3 of 3 tests): `permission_denied` from backend-mock's real Room after the grant is withdrawn, `rebase_failed` and `invalid_op` answers, and a `rebase` answer whose old identifier is gone before the work is sent again (packages/engine/src/sync/session.ts, `#journalSettle`). packages/engine/test/fe-008-journal.test.ts (Node.js, `pnpm vitest run --project node`, 5 of 5 tests): criterion 1, each commit is in the journal at the moment `channel.submit` is called and the write precedes the send; criterion 3, the journal is empty once the commit is saved; criterion 5, the entry carries only commit members and no token-like field; criterion 6 is only partly shown, so it is not ticked (reviewer, round 3): packages/element/src/journal-idb.universal.test.ts (Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, `pnpm vitest run --project browser`, 15 of 15 tests) writes an entry, closes the handle and reads it back with a second handle in the same page, and the engine test leaves an unacknowledged commit in an in-memory journal after `detach()`. No test kills a page and opens the document again. The queue and the in-flight commit are journalled (fe-008 test 'holds the in-flight commit and the queue'). Lane w1 (2026-11-15), criteria 2 and 4: `packages/engine/test/fe-008-queue-and-outage.test.ts` (Node.js, backend-mock's real Room, in-memory journal, 3 of 3 tests): with one commit refused for good, a second never answered and more typed, the journal holds the in-flight commit under its identifier, the queue and the recovered edit together, and a later change of the queue is written while the other two stay; a commit the service answers with `unavailable` (action `retry`) reads `unsaved` with the connection online, stays in the journal and is sent again under the same identifier, and reads `saved` with the journal empty once it is confirmed; `busy` retries never read `unsaved`. The connection-down half of criterion 4 is `packages/engine/test/fe-008-unsaved.test.ts`. The recovered edits in the browser's store: `packages/element/src/journal-idb.ts` (`setRecovered`, `readRecovered`) and `packages/element/e2e/fe-010-recovered-reopen.spec.ts` (not re-run by this lane).

**Notes:** Lane w1 (2026-11-17): since FE-009 a new visit consumes the journal's commit (it sends it again or sets it aside), so `packages/element/e2e/fe-008-journal-reopen.spec.ts` now reads the journal in the new tab before any editor opens the document, and no longer expects the reopened editor to show the server's document. Lane w1 (2026-11-15): criteria 2 and 4 ticked; all six are ticked. Criterion 2: recovered edits have been written to the journal since FE-010 (`SyncSession.#recordRecovered`); this lane added the test that shows the three together. Criterion 4, the half that was open: the lane applied the spec's own text, 06 §8 (failure modes, "PostgreSQL unavailable": rooms reject with `unavailable`, the journal keeps the work and the indicator shows "unsaved"). `SyncSession` now reports a `service` event when a commit of ours is answered `unavailable` and again when one is acknowledged, refused for another reason or set aside, and the core reads `unsaved` meanwhile. Only `unavailable` counts; `busy` and `rate_limited` still read `saving`. A commit that gets no answer at all still reads `saving` until the acknowledgement timeout reconnects the channel; that case is not in the spec's table and was left as it was. Not run against a real PostgreSQL outage: the answer is injected at the channel. Earlier: Review F7. Not specified: a failed journal write (IndexedDB unavailable, private window, quota), two tabs on one document, shared computers, and whose work an entry is (Open questions). Resubmitting journalled work on the next visit is FE-009 and keeping rejected content is FE-010. The journal store and the flush on `visibilitychange` are FE's; the save-state indicator is UX's (UX-016). This story absorbed SYN-025 during the cross-check. Second review (2026-11-06, SPK-027): SR-M33 confirms the gaps and proposes a "not protected" save state when the journal cannot write, one tab owning a document's journal through a lock, and a host call that clears the journal at sign-out; SR-M9: under ADR 0019 an access change must also purge journal entries and recovered edits that hold content now hidden ([review](../review/2026-11-06-second-review.md)). Round 3 (lane U-FE-001-016, 2026-11-13): criteria 1, 3 and 5 are ticked (see Evidence); criterion 6 was ticked by the lane and unticked by the reviewer (store-level evidence only; it needs an element run that kills a real page and opens the document again, with FE-009 for the read on return). Criterion 2 stays open: recovered edits join the journal only with FE-010. Criterion 4 stays open: the journal-failure path reads unsaved (packages/engine/test/fe-008-journal.test.ts, test 2). Lane V-FE-001-016 (2026-11-13): the connection-down half is tested in packages/engine/test/fe-008-unsaved.test.ts (Node.js, 1 of 1 test: a held commit reads saving while online, unsaved once the channel reports `resuming`, saving again when it is `live`, and saved with the journal empty once the service confirms it). The other half, a commit the service cannot answer while the channel is online, is open: such a commit reads saving until the acknowledgement timeout (SyncSession limits, a proposed default) makes the channel reconnect; whether it should read unsaved at once, and which answer counts as "cannot acknowledge", is not decided here. The default journal is an IndexedDB store opened by the element when the browser gives one (packages/element/src/journal-idb.ts; decision recorded in docs/adr/0300-lane-U-FE-001-016.md). Not decided: two tabs on one document, and a failed remove after an acknowledgement (it leaves a stale entry for FE-009 to deduplicate). Round 3 fix (lane U-FE-001-016): criterion 6 is ticked on the element e2e (see Evidence); on return the entry is only read, and resubmitting it is FE-009's. A commit that leaves flight without an acknowledgement now leaves the journal too: refused for good (`drop` and `reload` answers such as `permission_denied`, `rebase_failed`, `invalid_op`), set aside by a reload or a stale resume, or returned to the queue by `rebase` or a split (the queue is rewritten first, so the work is never in neither place). Refused content is then held in memory only, as recovered edits, until FE-010 keeps it under the purge rules of ADR 0019, so FE-009 cannot resubmit work the server refused. The IndexedDB journal stays on by default (ADR 0300); the sign-out purge SR-M33 proposes needs a host call in the element API and is not built.

### FE-009 Resubmit journalled work on the next visit

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 02 §2.2; 04 §2 (G4), §8, §9 · **Requirements:** R15, R7 · **Depends on:** FE-008, SYN-008, SYN-018, SYN-020, SYN-028

As an author, I want work I did not manage to confirm to be sent again when I return, so that nothing I typed is lost across a closed tab.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the journal holds unconfirmed commits for a document, when that document is opened again, then after the channel is open and `sync.caughtUp` has arrived the client resubmits them, in their original order, with their original commit identifiers.
- [x] Given a commit was accepted by the server before the tab closed and its acknowledgement was lost, when it is resubmitted, then the change appears once in the document, because the deduplication rule recognises the identifier, whether or not the first copy was stored.
- [x] Given the resume answer reports a journalled commit as `accepted`, when catch-up streams it, then the client treats it as acknowledged and does not resubmit it.
- [x] Given the answer is `catchUp: "reload"`, the work no longer applies, or the journal was written under a different incarnation of the log, when the client replaces its confirmed document, then the affected work moves to recovered edits (FE-010) instead of being dropped.
- [x] Given a journal entry whose commit is now rejected with `permission_denied`, `rebase_failed` or `invalid_op`, when it is resubmitted, then it is handled as in SYN-020 and its content is kept as recovered edits where the table says so.
- [x] Given a journal entry written for another document, when this document is opened, then the entry is not submitted to it.
- [ ] Given the fault-injection run of the phase 1b exit criterion (dropped connections, a killed process, a closed tab), when it ends, then nothing typed is missing and nothing is applied twice.

**Evidence (lane w1, 2026-11-17):** criteria 1 to 6. Source: `packages/engine/src/sync/restore.ts` (`readRestorePlan`, `restoreBase`), `packages/engine/src/core.ts` (`attach` option `restore`; `#restoreUnconfirmed` puts the journal's commit back as the in-flight commit under its own identifier and the queue behind it, out of the undo history, and returns what does not apply), `packages/engine/src/sync/session.ts` (`setAside`; the journal entry carries the log incarnation and the queue the `seq` it was made on), `packages/engine/src/sync/journal.ts` and `packages/element/src/journal-idb.ts` (`incarnation`, `queueAt`), `packages/element/src/session.ts` (reads the journal first, loads the content pinned at the commit's `base`, opens the channel with a resume at that `seq` naming the commit as in flight, and hands the plan to the core). Node.js, backend-mock's real Room, a journal that outlives the visits (`pnpm exec vitest run --project node packages/engine/test/fe-009-resubmit.test.ts packages/engine/test/fe-009-fault-injection.test.ts`, 2026-11-17: 13 passed; the whole engine and element node suites: 1449 passed): criterion 1 'sends it after the channel is live, under its own identifier, and the journal is empty after', 'puts the queue back behind the commit and sends both in their original order'; criterion 2 'applies the commit once when it was waiting to be stored and the client sends it again', 'rebases the work over what another person wrote between the visits'; criterion 3 'finds the stored commit in the catch-up stream and does not send it again' (the room stored it, the answer was lost; the second visit sends nothing and the text is in the document once); criterion 4 'a log of another incarnation answers reload, and the work is set aside, not dropped', 'content that cannot be loaded at the commit base sets the work aside and loads the head', 'a queue made after another commit moved the document goes to recovered edits, not to wrong positions'; criterion 5 `invalid_op`, `rebase_failed` and `permission_denied` (the real Room, after the grant was withdrawn between the visits), each kept as recovered edits and gone from the journal; criterion 6 'reads nothing for a document the journal has no entry for, and leaves the other entry alone'. Browser, the element with its default IndexedDB journal: `packages/element/e2e/fe-009-resubmit-reopen.spec.ts`, 'criterion 1' (the element is destroyed with a commit that never reached the service; a new element on the same document sends it again; the journal empties; the text is in the service's document once) and 'criterion 4' (a tab killed with the commit unconfirmed, a new tab whose service is another log: the work is not sent and is kept as recovered edits), run on 2026-11-17 on the development machine with `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts fe-009-resubmit-reopen fe-008-journal-reopen fe-011-visibility-flush --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit --retries=0`.

**Evidence (lane w1, 2026-11-18, decision 47 of `docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md`: unsent work of a returning user is resubmitted only for the same signed-in user, otherwise discarded after a warning; no criterion of this story names it, and it closes the open question below):** `packages/engine/src/sync/journal.ts` (`JournalEntry.user`, `QueueAt.user`), `packages/engine/src/sync/session.ts` (the session writes `opts.user`, which `core.attach` takes from `at.user`, i.e. `welcome.user.id`, with each commit and queue), `packages/element/src/journal-idb.ts`, `packages/engine/src/sync/restore.ts` (`discardForeignWork`: removes the commits and the queue whose user is another; work with no user written, from before this rule, is kept because nothing says whose it is), `packages/element/src/session.ts` (runs it before `readRestorePlan`, then `noticeForeignWork`), `packages/engine/src/core.ts` (`noticeForeignWork`: a refusal notice with the new reason `other-user`, shown in the status bar as "Unsent work from an earlier visit belonged to another person and was discarded. It was not sent."; it names nothing). `packages/engine/test/fe-009-user-binding.test.ts` (4 tests, Node.js, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node <file>`: 4 passed, 2026-11-18: the user is written with the commit and the queue; the same user's work stays and another user's goes; a queue of another user goes, a journal with no user is kept; the warning has no scopes) and `packages/element/e2e/fe-009-resubmit-reopen.spec.ts` ('with decision 47': Ada's unconfirmed commit is in IndexedDB, Bob opens the document next in the same profile, the notice appears, the journal is empty, the text is not in the service's document, not shown to Bob and not in his recovered edits; `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=58009 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/fe-009-resubmit-reopen.spec.ts packages/element/e2e/fe-008-journal-reopen.spec.ts packages/element/e2e/fe-011-visibility-flush.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit --retries=0 --workers=1`: 15 passed, 2026-11-18). The allow-list of `packages/engine/test/fe-008-journal.test.ts` gained `user`.

**Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-18):** criterion 7 stays unticked and is blocked: it asks for the fault-injection run of the phase 1b exit criterion (11 §1b: two users with different grants and one fill-only participant for an hour under dropped connections, a killed process and a database outage), and that run is BE-080, which is not built (its Notes: no package holds the engine's `DocumentCore`, the `@docedit/backend-dep` backend and the real service together, the harness has no PDF export step, and the nightly job is root configuration). No decision of 2026-11-15 or 2026-11-17 speaks to it; decision 47 answered the user binding below, which is built. Nothing was added for the run here: the client half is the property test named in the earlier Notes. Earlier Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-17): Criterion 7 stays unticked. `packages/engine/test/fe-009-fault-injection.test.ts` is a property test (fast-check, 30 runs of one to four random visits, each ending with a commit unanswered or its answer lost, a page hidden and closed, another person writing between visits): after a last visit has drained, every word typed is in the server's document once or in the recovered edits once, and the journal is empty (with the restore left out the property fails at the second run). That is the client's half only. The exit criterion of 11 §1b is two users with different grants, one fill-only participant, an hour under dropped connections, a killed process and a database outage, ending with identical documents; no such run was made. Decisions taken, proposed and not decided: only the oldest journal commit is put back (the client keeps one commit in flight, so a second can exist only after a failed removal; it stays in the journal); the queue is put back only when it was made on the same confirmed `seq` as the commit's `base`, otherwise it is kept as recovered edits (the journal did not record which remote commits it was rebased over); a set-aside item has the reason `stale`, or `reload` when the server answers `reload`; whose entries a returning user may resubmit (user binding) was the open question here; decision 47 settled it and it is built (see the Evidence of 2026-11-18). Two end-to-end specs of other stories had to change: `fe-008-journal-reopen.spec.ts` and `fe-011-visibility-flush.spec.ts` read the journal after the kill in a new tab and, before this story, then opened the document and expected the entry still to be there; now an open consumes the entry, so they read it before any editor opens the document. The allow-list of `fe-008-journal.test.ts` gained `incarnation`. `fe-005-suspend-resume.spec.ts` ('a suspended element takes its listeners and observers off the page') fails in Chromium on this branch because of the `resize` listener that `packages/engine/src/view/web.ts` adds for the peers' carets (SYN-036, committed 2026-11-15); it is not changed by this story and was not fixed here.

**Notes:** Whose entries a returning user may resubmit (user binding) is in Open questions. The resubmission criteria of the story merged into FE-008 during the cross-check are here. Lane V-FE-001-016 (2026-11-13), blocked: resubmission on open was not built then. The sync session never reads the journal when a document opens (`journal.read` has no caller in packages/engine/src/sync/session.ts), so a commit journalled before a kill stays in IndexedDB and is never sent. packages/element/e2e/fe-009-resubmit-reopen.spec.ts ('FE-009: journalled work is resubmitted once', marked `fixme` with the reason) types into a held channel, kills the tab, opens the document again and waits for the journal to empty: it fails on Chromium, Edge, Firefox and WebKit, because the journal keeps the typed text. Criteria 1 to 6 stay open. Building it needs the in-flight commit restored from the journal under its original identifier, with the queue, after `sync.caughtUp` (state.ts and session.ts); that is an engine change for its owner, not a test change. The user binding in Open questions is still undecided.

### FE-010 Keep recovered edits

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 02 §2.2; 04 §8; 05 §8.1 (`de-recovered-edits`); 07 §9; 13 §9; PRODUCT principle 4 · **Requirements:** R15, R3 · **Depends on:** FE-008, FE-009, FE-037, UX-017, UX-019, PRM-024, PRM-028

As an author, I want content that was rejected, could not be re-applied or was dropped by a rebase to be set aside where I can find it, so that I never lose text silently.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a commit is rejected with `permission_denied`, when the client discards the rejected steps, then the discarded content is written to the journal as recovered edits and appears in the recovered-edits pane with the region it was meant for, and later local steps that still apply stay in the document and in the send queue (07 §9).
- [x] Given journalled work does not apply after `sync.reset` or a reload, when the client rebuilds, then that work becomes recovered edits.
- [x] Given a rebase drops a local step because another user removed the content it applied to, when the rebase completes, then the typed text is kept as recovered edits and the user is told (04 §8).
- [x] Given recovered edits exist, when the tab is closed and opened again, then they are still available.
- [x] Given recovered edits exist, when the pane or a host asks for them, then the core returns each as a CDM fragment with its reason (rejected, unsent, dropped) and the region it was meant for.
- [x] Given content is set aside or the count of recovered edits changes, when the client raises `de-recovered-edits`, then `detail` carries `{ count }` and never content.

**Evidence:** packages/element/test/session.test.ts 'raises de-recovered-edits { count } when the core sets work aside' (the core's recovered view moves to 1, repeats 1, then 2; the host receives exactly `{ count: 1 }` and `{ count: 2 }`, with no items or content); src/session.ts (the subscription to the recovered view). Node.js, run 2026-11-13: the file passes 15 of 15 tests. Lane U-FE-001-016 (round 4, run 2026-11-14 UTC): packages/element/e2e/fe-010-recovered-reopen.spec.ts 'FE-010 criteria 1 and 4' (PostgreSQL not used. The reviewer's reruns (round 4) failed in Firefox in 3 of 4 runs because the click and `End` left the caret in the heading, outside the legal region, so nothing was refused. Round 4 fix, run 2026-11-14 UTC on the shared development machine: the caret is now put at the end of the legal paragraph through the harness's `caretIn`, the test waits until both the editor's selection and the browser's selection are in the paragraph that starts "Liability is limited", and it checks after typing that the text is in that paragraph and not in the heading; it also waits until all of the typed text is set aside, because under load the held commit can carry only the first key and the later keys are refused in later commits (WebKit failed 2 of 5 on that before the change). With `--retries=0`: all four projects with `--repeat-each=5` and 4 workers, 20 of 20 pass; Firefox and WebKit with `--repeat-each=5 --workers=1`, 10 of 10 pass; Firefox alone with `--repeat-each=5 --workers=1` before the WebKit change, 5 of 5 pass. In the test, Ada types into the legal region, her commit is held at the channel and journalled, her grant is withdrawn, the Room refuses it with `permission_denied`, the set-aside text is in the `recovered` store of `docedit-journal`, the tab is closed, and a new tab on the same document raises `de-recovered-edits` with the stored count); packages/engine/test/fe-010-recovered-journal.test.ts (the port round trip, with fe-008 journal tests: 10 of 10 pass in Node.js); packages/element/src/journal-idb.universal.test.ts (15 of 15 pass in the browser project, Chromium, but it has no test of the `recovered` store, so it is not evidence for this criterion); src/sync/session.ts (`#restoreRecovered`, `#recordRecovered`), src/sync/journal.ts (optional `setRecovered` and `readRecovered`), packages/element/src/journal-idb.ts (version 2, `recovered` store).
Lane w1 (2026-11-17), Node.js on Windows 11: `packages/engine/test/fe-010-recovered-edits.test.ts` (7 of 7 pass). Criterion 1: a `permission_denied` item from the real Room carries `kind: 'rejected'` and `region: region:<id>`, the refused text leaves the region, and a step queued behind it stays in the document and is sent (`saveState` `saved`); the pane shows the region by title from the same item shape (packages/ui/e2e/rejection-explained.spec.ts and packages/ui/test/rejection-words.test.ts, PRM-024, not rerun here). Criterion 2: `reload` and `reset` at the state machine give items with reason `reload` or `reset`, kind `unsent` and the region of the first step (the reload through the journal is packages/engine/test/fe-009-resubmit.test.ts, FE-009 criterion 4). Criterion 3: two people through the mock service, Ada's removal of the text stored first, Grace's step dropped by the rebase, the item has reason `rebase`, kind `dropped`, text `zz`, region `body`; the notice is packages/ui/e2e/notices.spec.ts 'a change dropped by a rebase is told' (new; run with `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57211 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts packages/ui/e2e/notices.spec.ts --retries=0`: 12 of 12 passed, Chromium, Firefox and WebKit; the item there is a shaped item given to the shell, as in the other cases of that file). The fifth criterion in the list (the fragment; the Notes call it criterion 6): `RecoveredItem` (packages/engine/src/api.ts) now has `kind` (`rejected`, `dropped`, `unsent`), `fragment` (a `CdmFragment` of what the steps inserted, null for a deletion) and `region`; src/recovered-fragment.ts builds the fragment, src/sync/state.ts (`Discarded.region`, `regionOfFirst`) finds the region on the document each step was made on, src/regions.ts `scopeOfPosition`, src/content.ts `fragmentOfSlice`.

**Notes:** The pane that lists them is UX's; the wording that explains a rejection is PRM's. The pane and the journal belong to UX and FE; this story fixes what the permission path and the rebase put into them. This story absorbed PRM-025 during the cross-check. Packet round 2 (lane FE-001-013, 2026-11-13): criterion 5 ticked on the session test above, where the count comes from a stub core view, so the core's own counting stays with the engine. Criteria 1, 2 and 4 need the journal (FE-008, not built). Criterion 3 has engine evidence for keeping the text (packages/engine/test/sync-state.test.ts 'a local step whose content another user removed is dropped and kept as recovered edit'), but the user being told is shown only through element tests with a stub core, so it stays open. Criterion 6 stays open: `RecoveredItem` (packages/engine/src/api.ts) carries reason, commit, text and seq, but no CDM fragment and no region. Lane V-FE-001-016 (2026-11-13): criterion 1 stays open. The e2e run shows the refusal, the journal write and the count, but not the region the edits were meant for, the pane entry, or that later local steps stay in the document and the queue; the rebuild and text-keeping of criterion 2 and 3 are unchanged. Criterion 4 is ticked on the e2e run above. Lane U-FE-001-016 (round 4): the journal (FE-008) is built, so the reason for criteria 1, 2 and 4 "not built" is gone. Criterion 4 is ticked (see Evidence). Criterion 1 stays open: the refusal is journalled and survives a reopen, but the pane showing the region it was meant for and the later local steps staying in the document and the queue are not proven. Criterion 2 stays open: nothing rebuilds journalled work after `sync.reset` or a reload. Criterion 3 stays open: the typed text is kept, but the message to the person is shown only by element tests with a stub core. Recovered items are kept until a person dismisses them, and no dismiss action exists yet (ADR 0300, decisions 8 to 11). Lane w1 (2026-11-17): all criteria ticked. Two limits, stated so that the ticks are read correctly. The hosts' path to the fragments is the core's `recovered` view (`DocumentCore.recovered`); `<doc-editor>` does not expose it as a method, and the spec names none, so a host that only has the element gets `de-recovered-edits { count }`. The word `unsent` is mapped from the reasons `reload`, `reset` and `stale`, and `dropped` from `rebase`; the spec does not list the three words, so this mapping is proposed here (`recoveredKind` in packages/engine/src/recovered-fragment.ts). The region of an item written to the journal before this change is null, except for a `permission_denied` item, whose region is the scope the server named.

### FE-011 Flush the journal when the page is hidden

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | S | must | Author |

**Spec:** 02 §2.2; review F7 · **Requirements:** R15 · **Depends on:** FE-008

As an author, I want the journal complete whenever the page becomes hidden, so that switching tabs, locking a phone or closing the tab leaves nothing behind.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the page becomes hidden, when `visibilitychange` fires, then every pending journal write is started and the journal holds all unconfirmed work.
- [x] Given a keystroke is made immediately before the page is hidden, when the page is hidden, then that keystroke is in the journal.
- [x] Given the tab is closed with no `pagehide` handler running, when the document is opened again, then the work flushed on `visibilitychange` is recoverable (the component does not rely on `pagehide`).
- [x] Given the page is hidden, when the flush runs, then no WebSocket send is attempted as the only copy of the work.

**Evidence:** packages/engine/test/fe-011-journal-flush.test.ts (Node.js, `pnpm vitest run --project node`, 1 of 1 test, 2 of 4 criteria): with a journal whose writes wait on a gate, the keystroke made just before `core.flushJournal()` (the call the element makes on `visibilitychange` when the page is hidden) is in the journal once the flush resolves, the flush does not resolve before the write, and nothing is sent before its write is durable. Lane V-FE-001-016 (2026-11-13): packages/element/e2e/fe-011-visibility-flush.spec.ts 'FE-011: a page hidden with unconfirmed work keeps it in the journal and after a kill' (Playwright, `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=57001 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/fe-011-visibility-flush.spec.ts --trace=off`, Chromium, Edge, Firefox and WebKit: 4 passed): Ada's commit is held unconfirmed, the page is hidden with the simulated visibility state and a real `visibilitychange` event, the journal holds the typed text, the tab is killed (renderer crash through CDP in Chromium and Edge, close without unload in Firefox and WebKit), and a new tab reads the same journal back. Criterion 3 is ticked on this run; the element has no `pagehide` listener (packages/element/src/session.ts `#watchEditor`). Lane w1 (2026-11-17), criterion 1: packages/engine/test/fe-011-journal-flush.test.ts 'every write requested is started, and the journal holds a commit in flight and the work after it' (`pnpm exec vitest run --project node packages/engine/test/fe-011-journal-flush.test.ts`: 2 passed): a commit's write and the queue writes of two later keystrokes are held at a gate, `flushJournal()` does not resolve while they are held, and once released the journal holds the commit that the service never confirmed and a stored queue with the later work, with the commit sent once and only after its write. The element's call on `visibilitychange` is the browser run of 2026-11-13 above. All four criteria are ticked.

**Notes:** `pagehide` is unreliable, especially on mobile (02 §2.2). A REST commit endpoint usable by a keep-alive request is an open issue in 05 §12. Round 3 (lane U-FE-001-016, 2026-11-13): criteria 2 and 4 are ticked on the engine test above. Criteria 1 and 3 stay open. The listener is in packages/element/src/session.ts (`#watchEditor`), but no browser run hides a real page to fire `visibilitychange`, so the element wiring is untested. The flush itself is FE-008's journal (packages/engine/src/sync/journal.ts). Lane V-FE-001-016 (2026-11-13): a real hidden page cannot be made in Playwright (a second page brought to the front leaves this one `visible` on Chromium; measured in the e2e run), so the browser test simulates the hidden state and dispatches a real `visibilitychange` event. Criterion 1 stays open: "every pending journal write is started" needs a write that is still pending when the page is hidden, which no browser test creates; the engine test covers the wait.

### FE-012 Wait for confirmation with `flush`

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | S | must | Integrating developer |

**Spec:** 02 §2.2; 04 §8 ("Flush"); 05 §8.1 · **Requirements:** R15, R6 · **Depends on:** FE-008, SYN-018, PRO-076, PRO-083, PRO-084, PRO-085, PRO-086, PRO-087

As an integrating developer, I want `flush({ timeoutMs })` to tell me when everything is confirmed, so that I can navigate away safely.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given nothing is in flight or queued, when `flush({ timeoutMs })` is called, then the promise resolves at once.
- [x] Given unconfirmed commits, when they are all confirmed before the timeout, then the promise resolves.
- [x] Given commits are still unconfirmed at the timeout, when the timeout elapses, then the promise rejects with the number of unconfirmed commits and the work stays in the journal.
- [x] Given `flush` is called while the channel is reconnecting, when the channel reopens and the commits are acknowledged, then the promise resolves only after confirmation, not after reconnection.
- [x] Given a host navigates away after a rejected `flush`, when the document is opened again, then the unconfirmed work is resubmitted (FE-009).

**Evidence:** packages/element/test/host-api.test.ts 'flush (PRO-083)' (criterion 1: `flush({ timeoutMs: 50 })` with nothing in flight resolves, against the headless core); packages/element/test/host-api.test.ts 'the element methods over the session (PRO-083, PRO-084, PRO-085)' (criterion 2: a local edit is sent, `flush({ timeoutMs: 5000 })` resolves after the mock backend acknowledges it, and the confirmed `seq` and document match the server's). Node.js, run 2026-11-13. Lane U-FE-001-016 (round 3, 2026-11-13), criterion 3: packages/engine/test/fe-012-flush-journal.test.ts (Node.js): a commit the server never answers; `flush({ timeoutMs: 50 })` rejects with `count` 1, and the journal still holds that one commit. Lane w1 (2026-11-15), criterion 4: packages/engine/test/fe-012-flush-reconnect.test.ts (Node.js, mock backend, 1 passed): the first channel never answers a commit, `core.reconnect()` puts the connection in `reconnecting`, and `flush({ timeoutMs: 5000 })` is called then; the test holds the reopening and then the second channel's answer, and the promise is still pending while reconnecting and after the connection is `online` again with the commit still in the journal; it resolves once the answer is released, the journal is then empty and the service holds the text at `seq` + 1. Lane w1 (2026-11-18), criterion 5: packages/engine/test/fe-012-flush-resubmit.test.ts (Node.js, backend-mock with the real Room, an in-memory journal that outlives the visits, 1 passed): the first visit's channel never answers the commit, `flush({ timeoutMs: 50 })` rejects with `count` 1 and the journal holds the commit; the core is detached as a closed tab leaves it; the second visit does what the element's session does (`readRestorePlan`, content pinned at the commit's `base`, a resume that names the commit as in flight, `attach` with the plan), and the commit that goes out is the first visit's, under the same identifier; the text reaches the server once and the journal is empty after `flush`. FE-009 (its commit a48ffbee on this branch) built the resubmission; this test is the FE-012 path through it.

**Notes:** This story absorbed SYN-024 during the cross-check. Packet round 4 (2026-11-13): criteria 1 and 2 ticked for the channel-attached path only. Criterion 3 stays open: the rejection with the count is not tested, and the work staying in the journal needs FE-008 (not built). Criterion 4 stays open: it needs a reconnecting channel (FE-005). Criterion 5 is FE-009. Round 8 (element packet, 2026-11-13): packages/element/test/flush.test.ts runs the rejection on a headless core with one local edit and no channel: it rejects at the timeout with `count` 1 and the edit stays visible. The count comes from the engine's `flush` (`packages/engine/src/core.ts`), which counts the in-flight commit and the queue as one each, not the commits the queue would send; that is for the engine's owner (ADR 0109, round 8). Criteria 1 and 2 stay as they are: the journal is FE-008. Round 3 (lane U-FE-001-016): criterion 3 is ticked on the engine test above (the journal half is FE-008, now built); the count is still in-flight plus queue, as the round 8 note says, for the engine's owner (ADR 0300, lane U-FE-001-016). Criterion 4 stays open: it needs a reconnecting channel (FE-005). Lane w1 (2026-11-15): criterion 4 is ticked on the engine test above; no source change was needed, because `flush` waits on the sync state and not on the connection. Criterion 5 stays open and is blocked on FE-009: nothing reads the journalled commits back when a document is opened again (`CommitJournal.read` has no caller in `packages/engine/src` or `packages/element/src`), and every criterion of FE-009 is unticked. Building the resubmission here would be FE-009 itself, so it was not done. Lane w1 (2026-11-18): FE-009 is now built (criteria 1 to 6 ticked there, the fault-injection run apart), so criterion 5 is ticked on the test named in Evidence; the criterion that FE-009 leaves open (the fault-injection run of the phase 1b exit) is not part of this one. All criteria of FE-012 are ticked.

## Shadow root, styles, Trusted Types, theming, overlays, keyboard and events (02 §3)

### FE-013 Create the shadow root and manage focus explicitly

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1a | S | must | Author |

**Spec:** 02 §3; ADR 0002 · **Requirements:** R2 · **Depends on:** FE-003

As an author, I want a click on the page surface to put the caret in the document, so that I can type at once.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the element is constructed, when its shadow root is inspected, then `mode` is `"open"` and `delegatesFocus` is `false`.
- [x] Given a Playwright locator, when it addresses an element inside the editor, then it pierces the open root without extra configuration.
- [x] Given the page surface is clicked, when focus settles, then the editing surface has focus and the first toolbar button does not.
- [x] Given `focus()` is called on the element, when it returns, then the editing surface has focus with its last selection restored.

**Evidence:** packages/element/e2e/element.spec.ts 'FE-013 shadow root and focus' (a click on the page surface focuses the editing surface, not a toolbar button; focus() returns the caret to where it was and typing continues there) and s1-shadow-root.spec.ts (open root, delegatesFocus false, Playwright locators pierce it), in Chromium, Edge, Firefox and WebKit; src/doc-editor.ts. Run of 2026-11-06, 17:39 UTC, the whole element suite in four browsers: 780 passed, 0 failed, 1 flaky (passed on retry), 455 skipped by design.

**Notes:** An open root is required by ProseMirror's focus handling and by testing tools; it is not a security boundary (10 §3.1).

### FE-014 Share one constructable stylesheet between instances

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1a | M | must | Integrating developer |

**Spec:** 02 §3; ADR 0002 · **Requirements:** R2 · **Depends on:** FE-002, SPK-015

As an integrating developer, I want the component's CSS attached as a shared constructable stylesheet, so that a strict content security policy does not block it and many instances stay cheap.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the compiled CSS, when the module loads, then it is loaded once into one constructable `CSSStyleSheet`.
- [x] Given two editor instances on one page, when both have mounted, then their `adoptedStyleSheets` contain the same sheet object.
- [x] Given a page whose policy has no `unsafe-inline` for styles and no nonce, when the editor mounts and is used, then no style violation is reported and the normal path creates no `<style>` element.
- [x] Given every dependency that sets styles from script (including assignments to `element.style` and `style.cssText` by the editing view), when the strict-policy page is exercised, then each is covered by the outcome of SPK-015 and any violation is listed with its workaround.

**Evidence:** packages/element/e2e/element.spec.ts 'two instances have their own cores and share one constructable stylesheet' (the same CSSStyleSheet object in both roots, no style element) and pdf-viewer.spec.ts 'works under a strict host policy' (the strict baseline of spike S2, no unsafe-inline and no nonce: no violation with the editor mounted), s2-styles.spec.ts (adopted sheet and nonce fallback), in Chromium, Edge, Firefox and WebKit; spike S2 report §4 for the dependencies that set styles from script; src/styles.ts (one sheet with the element's and the shell's CSS). Run of 2026-11-06, 17:39 UTC, the whole element suite in four browsers: 780 passed, 0 failed, 1 flaky (passed on retry), 455 skipped by design.

**Notes:** Assigning `style.cssText` is disputed under CSP and ProseMirror's view does it (02 §3); S2 decides.

### FE-015 Fall back to a nonce style element

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | S | should | Integrating developer |

**Spec:** 02 §3; ADR 0002 (after the first review) · **Requirements:** R2 · **Depends on:** FE-014

As an integrating developer, I want a fallback for browsers or policies where constructable stylesheets fail, so that the editor is still styled.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given constructable stylesheets are unavailable or adopting the sheet throws, when the editor mounts, then it creates its own `<style>` element in the shadow root carrying the same CSS and a `nonce` taken from `config`.
- [x] Given a page whose policy allows styles only with that nonce, when the fallback is used, then the editor renders identically to the normal path (visual regression) and reports no violation.
- [x] Given the build configuration, when it is inspected, then Svelte's injected-style mode is not used, because it sets no nonce.
- [x] Given the normal path works, when the editor mounts, then the fallback is not used and no `<style>` element exists.

**Evidence:** packages/element/test/styles.test.ts 'FE-015' (six tests, run 2026-11-13: with constructable sheets missing, or adopting the sheet throwing, the root gets one `<style data-de-styles>` with the nonce from `config` and the component CSS as its text; the forced fallback does the same; with no nonce the result is `none` and nothing is added; a second attach adds no second element; the normal path adopts one shared sheet and creates no `<style>` on two roots); packages/element/test/source.test.ts 'FE-016' (every component build compiles with `css: "external"`, so Svelte's injected-style mode is never used); packages/element/e2e/fe-015-nonce-render.spec.ts 'FE-015 nonce fallback renders identically to the adopted sheet' (run 2026-11-13 by lane V-FE-001-016 with `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts` and the file, Chromium, Edge, Firefox and WebKit: 4 passed; the element screenshot is pixel-identical between the adopted and the nonce-style paths, and no violation is reported before the screenshot).

**Notes:** Phase inferred: the roadmap does not place it; it follows the S2 outcome. What the component reports when the fallback is needed but no nonce was supplied is not specified. Packet D1 (2026-11-13): criterion 2 stays open: no visual regression under the nonce policy has been run. The S2 page (e2e/s2-styles.spec.ts) checks the computed toolbar and region styles under that policy, but it was not run in this round. The 'adopting throws' case is tested with a stand-in root in Node.js, not in a browser. Lane U-FE-001-016 (2026-11-13): e2e/fe-015-nonce-render.spec.ts compares the element's screenshot on the adopted path and on the nonce fallback under a nonce-only policy: identical with no violation in Chromium and Firefox; Edge and WebKit report one unattributed `style-src-elem` inline violation, so the test was `fixme` there.
