Criterion 2 stays open: `lineBounds` gives the targets of Home and End, but the Shift extension belongs to the input bridge (FE-098), and no key handler is built. Criterion 3 ticked in round 8 (ADR 0120 item 36): "a page of the layout" is read as one layout page, so `movePage` moves to the nearest line at the caret's height on the next or previous page. A screenful would need the painter's visible height, and is not built. Criterion 5 stays open: the rule that the commands stay in the body is FE-032's, and the movement here is over body lines only (headers, footers and footnotes are not painted in the page view's caret path). A wrapped line's End lands on its last position, `to - 1`, and stays on that line, since no affinity is recorded (docs/adr/0120-layout-backlog-batch.md, item 28). Lane U-FE-097-112 (2026-11-13), criterion 5 stays open: `packages/element/e2e/s1-shadow-root.spec.ts` 'Ctrl+Home, Ctrl+End, arrows and Backspace never leave the body' passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (`-g "never leave the body"`, 3 passed), but that runs the web view's editing surface; the page view's caret path, which this criterion is about, is not built (FE-098, FE-101). Criterion 2 stays open for the same reason: the Shift extension is the input bridge's (FE-098), and no key handler is built.

**Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-18):** Criteria 2 and 5 ticked on the key logic in `page-keys.ts`, which the input bridge (FE-098) will call: it takes the layout tree and a selection, not a DOM event handler, so no browser run backs these two ticks. The bridge itself, and the mounting of the page view, are FE-098 and FE-101. Not built here: Left and Right (they need block text lengths, which the tree does not hold, as `packages/perf/src/s9/navigate.ts` shows), and the macOS forms (Cmd+Up, Cmd+Left), which the story does not name.

### FE-098 Type into the page view through the input bridge

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 3 | L | must | Author |

**Spec:** 02 §9.6; ADR 0003, 0005; 11 §6 · **Requirements:** R1, R11 · **Depends on:** FE-096, FE-097, FE-123, SPK-022

As an author, I want to type, compose with an input method and paste in the page view, so that it is a full editing surface.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the bridge design chosen by S9, when the author types, then text arrives in the document with the right marks and position.
- [ ] Given the S1 input matrix (Japanese, Chinese and Korean composition, dead keys, an Android and an iOS keyboard), when it runs in the page view, then it passes, including with remote commits during composition and in sub-editors.
- [ ] Given an input-method window, when composition starts on a painted page, then the window appears at the caret.
- [ ] Given a word that appears once on a mounted page, when the browser's find-in-page searches it, then it finds exactly one match.
- [x] Given the EditContext API, when S9 decides whether to use it, then any use is a second pipeline that passes the same matrix; otherwise it is not used.
- [x] Given the bridge, when the author uses shortcuts, then the command registry works as in the web view.

**Evidence (lane w1, 2026-11-18):** criteria 1, 5 and 6. Criterion 1: `packages/element/e2e/fe-101-page-view.spec.ts` (Chromium and WebKit: text typed after a click arrives in the block at the caret; Ctrl+B then typing gives text that is bold in the document and painted bold in the page; Enter splits and Backspace joins; arrows and Shift by the layout) and `packages/engine/src/view/fe-098-page-input.test.ts` (8 tests: replace, delete, caret, key, step, read-only refusal). Criterion 5: `packages/element/test/fe-101-page-view.test.ts` reads the page view's sources and finds no `EditContext` (ADR 0380 item 3). Criterion 6: the same spec, Ctrl+B through the core's bindings and Alt+Q through the registry's command search, both from the bridge (`MountedEditor.pageShortcut`). Not ticked, with what was run: `packages/element/e2e/fe-098-bridge-composition.spec.ts` (Chromium, composition made with the DevTools protocol: a composition is a series of edits, the result is committed once, a remote commit to the block waits and is applied after it, and the bridge sits on the painted caret within 2 pixels).

**Notes:** The bridge may not be the whole document's accessibility tree, the input-method anchor at the caret and the key handler at once; S9 decides the design (review F1, 02 §9.6). Native spellcheck is lost in the page view; the proofing port answers it (UX). Touch selection handles and mobile editing in the page view are open (02 §15).

**Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-18):** Blocked; nothing new could be ticked. The design S9 chose is ADR 0380 (a textarea bridge holding a window of the current block, movement and clicks from the layout tree, EditContext deferred, find as the page's own command), and it is still proposed: the owner has not accepted it (it is not among the ADRs that decision 220 of docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md accepts), and its own text says FE-098 may start on it but not close on it. Criterion 1 would need the product bridge in the element and the page view mounted to type into; the only bridge is the prototype in `packages/perf/src/s9/` on a spike document, and the element mounts no painted pages (FE-095 and FE-101 Notes), so a test of typing with the right marks and position would run on a stand-in and not on the page view. Criterion 2 (the S1 input matrix in the page view) needs the bridge and real input methods; the operating-system input-method windows were not seen in S9 (SPK-022 Notes). Criterion 3 (the input-method window at the caret) needs a person with Microsoft IME, Pinyin, Korean IME and an Android or iOS keyboard. Criterion 4 (find-in-page finds one match) needs the browser's find bar in headed browsers; Playwright cannot drive it (SPK-022 criterion 5). Criterion 5 is a decision on EditContext, which ADR 0380 item 3 defers; it is met only once the owner accepts that. Criterion 6 (shortcuts through the command registry) needs the bridge in the element. What this lane added for the story: the key logic that the bridge will call for Home, End, Page keys, the start and end of the document, and the region clamp (`packages/layout/src/page-keys.ts`, FE-096 and FE-097). Needs from others: the owner's decision on ADR 0380, the page view's mounting (FE-101), a person to run the input-method checks. Re-checked 2026-11-18 (second pass, lane w1): decision 220 names spike choices S3 and S6 and ADRs 0020 and 0030 to 0052, not S9 or ADR 0380 (the w9 record), and no other decision or the closing paragraph of docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md mentions S9, the input bridge or EditContext, so the ADR is still not accepted; FE-101 has not mounted the page view either. Criterion 5 (EditContext not used) would be met by that ADR and is true of product code today (only the spike probe in `packages/perf/src/browser/s9-page.ts` names it), but it is left unticked until the owner accepts item 3.

**Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-18, the bridge):** built on ADR 0380 (proposed; the owner has not accepted it, so the story does not close on it) as `packages/element/src/page-view/page-view.ts` and `packages/engine/src/page-input.ts`. Criterion 2 stays open: the S1 matrix needs real input methods (Japanese, Chinese, Korean), dead keys and phone keyboards, and sub-editors; the DevTools composition above is a stand-in and covers none of them. Criterion 3 stays open: the geometry of the bridge is tested, the window of a real input method is not. Criterion 4 stays open and is likely to stay open on this design: the bridge holds a copy of the current block, which the browser's find may match as well as the painted line (ADR 0380 item 5 makes find the page's own command); no find has been run. Left and Right, and Ctrl with them, follow the block order; Up, Down, Page Up, Page Down, Home, End, Ctrl+Home and Ctrl+End come from the layout (FE-096, FE-097). Not built: a find command of the page, drag of text, the context menu from a click in the page, and the S1 text-reflow cases.

### FE-099 Read and edit the page view with a screen reader

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 3 | L | must | Author |

**Spec:** 02 §9.6, §10; 11 §6, §2 (S9) · **Requirements:** R11 · **Depends on:** FE-098, FE-093, FE-107, SPK-022

As an author who uses a screen reader, I want to read and edit in the page view, so that it is not a visual-only mode.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given the tested plan from S9, when the manual screen-reader passes it names are run, then a user can read by paragraph and heading, move the caret and edit text in the page view.
- [ ] Given fields and signature buttons on the overlay (FE-093), when a screen-reader user navigates, then they are reachable and announced with their labels.
- [ ] Given live regions (FE-107), when saving, rejection and presence events occur in the page view, then they are announced.
- [ ] Given the page view, when a user prefers the accessible layout, then the web view with the same content is available as the alternative.

**Notes:** What screen readers read and edit in the page view is decided by S9 (02 §10).

**Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-18):** Blocked; nothing new could be ticked. Criterion 1 is a manual pass with at least one screen reader per platform (NVDA or JAWS, VoiceOver, Orca, TalkBack) on the plan of S9 report section 5; none has been run (SPK-022 Notes), and none can be run by an agent on this machine. The plan itself is part of ADR 0380, which is proposed and not accepted by the owner, and the page view it applies to is not mounted in the element (FE-101), so there is nothing to read yet. Criteria 2 and 3 name the overlay (FE-093) and the live regions (FE-107) inside the page view; both exist as parts (`packages/painter/src/overlay.universal.test.ts`, `packages/element/src/live-region.ts` and `announcer.ts`), but no page view hosts them, and 'announced' is a screen-reader observation, not a DOM check. Criterion 4 needs the page view to offer the web view as the alternative (FE-101 offers the views; `selectView` in `packages/element/src/view-select.ts` falls back to `web` while `pageViewAvailable` is false). Needs from others: FE-101, the owner's decision on ADR 0380, and a person with the four screen readers.

**Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-18, second pass):** Blocked again; nothing new could be ticked. Checked against the decisions file and the code of this branch. Stale: the ADR. The run's instruction counts decisions 196 to 220 as accepting every proposed default, so the recommended option of ADR 0380 (the bridge is a window of the current block, the painted pages are `aria-hidden`, a hidden reflowing text alternative and a live region carry what a screen reader reads) is taken as accepted here; the file's closing paragraph does not list S9 as open. Still true, and not stale: (1) nothing is mounted. `grep` finds no caller of `paintTree` or `mountPageWindow` in `packages/element/src`, `packages/engine/src` or `packages/ui/src`; `doc-editor.ts` passes `pageViewAvailable: false` to `selectView` (line 636), so there is no page view to read, to navigate fields in, or to announce events from. (2) The product bridge and the hidden text alternative of ADR 0380 item 4 are not built: the only `BridgeSession` is the S9 prototype in `packages/perf/src/s9/bridge.ts`, used by `packages/perf/src/browser/s9-page.ts`. That is FE-098's work (nothing ticked there), not this story's, and a bridge built here would be FE-098 under another id. (3) The painter draws one face and one size for the whole document (`PaintSource.fontFamily` and `fontSizePt`, `packages/painter/src/painter.ts`), with no marks, so a page view built today could not show a screen-reader user bold, links or fields as the web view does. (4) Criterion 1 is a manual pass with a screen reader per platform on the plan of S9 report section 5; none has been run and none can be run by an agent on this machine. Criteria 2 and 3 say a screen-reader user reaches and hears controls and live announcements; the overlay (FE-093) and the live regions (FE-107) exist as parts, and their accessible names and roles can be checked in the accessibility tree once a page view hosts them, but what a screen reader says is not a DOM property, so these two would be ticked on that check only if the owner accepts it as the test; the question is recorded here, not decided. Criterion 4 needs a page view that offers the web view as the alternative. Needs from others: FE-098 (the bridge), FE-101 (the mount), the painter's runs, and a person with the screen readers.

**Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-18):** the page view is mounted (FE-101), so a screen-reader pass can be made; none has been, so nothing is ticked. What a reader will find: the bridge is a textarea with an accessible name (the editing surface's label) kept at the caret, and the painted lines are real text in the page that is not hidden from assistive technology. ADR 0380 item 4 plans the opposite (painted pages `aria-hidden` and a hidden reflowing text alternative with a live region that says line and block on each move); that is not built, so a reader may meet the bridge's one block and the painted lines at once. There is no live announcement of a caret move yet. Criterion 4: the web view is one value of `view` away (e2e `fe-101-page-view.spec.ts` switches both ways with the same core), but the shell has no control that a person uses to switch, so "when a user prefers the accessible layout" has no way in except the host setting the attribute. That is a UX decision, not made here.

### FE-100 Reconcile page breaks with the typeset result

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 3 | L | must | Author |

**Spec:** 02 §9.7; 09 §7, §7.1; 11 §6 · **Requirements:** R1, R5 · **Depends on:** FE-087, FE-088, EXP-055, EXP-057

As an author, I want the pages I see to match the PDF for text I have not touched since the last compile, so that I can trust page starts.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a `layout.json` for `seq` S that passes its schema, when the page view adopts it, then for paragraphs not edited since S the engine adopts the typesetter's line and page breaks, and uses its own for the rest.
- [x] Given the content between two reported page starts does not fit the editor's page, when the page is laid out, then the page may overflow slightly.
- [x] Given the overflow exceeds the tolerance, when the page is laid out, then the editor drops that pin and marks the page as estimated until the next compile.
- [x] Given `granularity` is `block`, when the page view adopts the file, then only block-level page starts are used; given `line`, then line starts are used as well.
- [ ] Given a test corpus, when reconciliation runs, then the on-screen page starts equal the PDF's.
- [x] Given a `layout.json` that fails validation, when it is received, then it is not used.

**Evidence:** Fixer round 2 (B group 1, 2026-11-13): criteria 2 and 3 are ticked again on the page pass. `packages/layout/src/paginate.ts` takes typeset page starts (`PaginateOptions.pins`: the pins and the tolerance): an adopted start begins a page, which holds its content up to the next typeset start below its body if need be; the overflow of each pinned page is measured on that layout (the depth of its lowest body fragment below the body); `judgePins` keeps or drops each pin; the document is laid out again with the kept pins, and the pages of a dropped pin's content carry `estimated: true` and are listed in `Pagination.reconciliation`. `packages/layout/src/engine.ts` passes them through `layoutFlow(doc, providers, { pins })`. `packages/layout/src/reconcile-layout.test.ts` (10 tests, passed 2026-11-13 with `pnpm exec vitest run packages/layout`, 301 passed): 'keeps the typeset start of B and lets page 1 overflow within the tolerance' and 'allows an overflow equal to the tolerance' (criterion 2); 'drops the pin of the page that overflows too far, lays it out with the editor breaks, and marks it' and 'drops a pin just above the tolerance' (criterion 3); a start inside a paragraph, unmatched starts, a validated `layout.json` through `adoptablePins` to the pages, and a fast-check property (200 runs) that pinned pagination ends, places every line once and keeps no pin beyond the tolerance. Earlier review text: criteria 2 and 3 were unticked by review (2026-11-13): no page is laid out with pins (the engine takes none, criterion 1), nothing measures the overflow of a page, and the tolerance has no source, so `judgePins` is a rule with no caller; its tests become evidence once layout adopts pins. Criteria 4 and 6 stay ticked as contracts of `adoptablePins` and `validateLayoutJson`, which are exported from the package. `packages/layout/src/layout-json.ts` (`validateLayoutJson`, the interim reading of 09 §7.1) and `packages/layout/src/reconcile.ts` (`adoptablePins`, `effectiveGranularity`, `judgePins`, `keptPins`, `estimatedPages`), with `packages/layout/src/layout-json.test.ts` and `packages/layout/src/reconcile.test.ts`, in Node.js (run of 2026-11-13: `pnpm vitest run` on the three new files, 30 tests passed; scoped `tsc --noEmit` clean for these files). Criterion 2: 'keeps a pin whose content fits, and a pin whose page overflows within the tolerance' and 'allows an overflow equal to the tolerance and drops one just above it'. Criterion 3: 'drops a pin beyond the tolerance and marks its page as estimated'. Criterion 4: 'uses line granularity only when the request and the file both say line' and 'uses block starts only when the file reports block granularity, whatever the request'. Criterion 6: the refusal tests of `layout-json.test.ts` (a file that fails any check gives no layout, and `adoptablePins` takes only a validated `LayoutJson`).

**Evidence (layout packet, round 5, criterion 1):** `packages/layout/src/line-starts.test.ts` (9 tests, Node.js, passing 2026-11-13): `breakParagraphAt` (`lines.ts`) cuts a paragraph at the given starts and starts a line at each, for random text and starts (200 seeded runs) and refuses starts out of range; `layoutFlow` with the `lines` option (`engine.ts`, `paragraphItem` in `items.ts`) starts a line at a typeset offset inside a paragraph, ignores a start on no text position, leaves the neighbour paragraph to the editor, and adopts the line starts of a validated `layout.json` through `adoptablePins` only for blocks not edited since `seq`. The page starts of the same file are the earlier fixer-round evidence.

**Notes:** 09 §7 calls level L1 "opt-in, from phase 3"; 02 §9.7 describes adoption without saying it is optional. The tolerance is "small" and has no value (Open questions). Second review (2026-11-06, SPK-027), SR-K6: 02 §9.7 now says adoption happens only when reconciliation is switched on (opt-in per tenant, from phase 3), so the criteria apply to tenants that opt in ([review](../review/2026-11-06-second-review.md)).

Layout packet, round 2 (lane layout-2, 2026-11-13): criteria 2, 3, 4 and 6 are met by the policy and the validator, with items 7 and 8 in docs/adr/0135-layout-2-backlog-batch.md (proposed), and the open adoption is item 10 there. The schema of `layout.json` is not published in the repository, so the validator is the interim reading of 09 section 7.1, and it refuses unknown keys. Criterion 1 is not met: the engine does not take pins yet, so the page view does not adopt the typesetter's breaks; `adoptablePins` gives the set it would use. Criterion 2 relies on the caller to measure how far the content between two starts overflows the editor's page; that measurement from the live tree is not built. The tolerance has no value in the spec (Open questions), so it is a required argument with no default. Criterion 5 needs a compiled corpus and the adopted breaks, and no browser run was made. Round 5 (layout packet, 2026-11-13): criterion 1 ticked on the engine side (Evidence above; decisions in docs/adr/0120-layout-backlog-batch.md, items 49 and 50, proposed). What the page view does with the adopted starts (the caller of `layoutFlow` in the element) is not built and is not checked by that test; criterion 5 stays open.

Fixer round 2 (B group 1, 2026-11-13): the page pass now adopts page starts and measures the overflow itself (see Evidence), so the two sentences above on pins and on measurement by the caller are out of date for page starts. Still open: criterion 1, because the page view (`painter`, the engine's commit log for the edited set) does not yet call `layoutFlow` with pins and line starts are not adopted; the tolerance's value, because no tenant setting for it exists in the protocol or the configuration (02 §9.7 says "small", open question 6), so `PinOptions.tolerance` is required and has no default. Decisions of the fixer round, not in the spec: the overflow is in points, measured as the depth of the page's lowest body fragment below its body, without the footnote area, and a held page does not make room for its footnotes; explicit page, column and section breaks inside a pinned range are honoured; the pages estimated for a dropped pin run from the page its content starts on to the page before the next typeset start; a start whose block or line is not laid out (a table, until FE-083 adds tables to the pins) is not adopted and is reported as unmatched.

Lane V-FE-097-112 (round 3, 2026-11-13): criterion 4 ticked. `adoptablePins` (`packages/layout/src/reconcile.ts`) now adopts at block granularity only the page starts at offset 0 (a start inside a block keeps the editor's break, and is listed in `keptOwnPages`). Evidence: `packages/layout/src/reconcile.test.ts` 'at block granularity adopts only block-level starts, and keeps the break of a start inside a block' and 'gives no line starts at block granularity, even when the file reports them' (now asserting pages 1 and 3), with `reconcile-layout.test.ts`, `line-starts.test.ts` and `layout-json.test.ts`, in Node.js (`pnpm exec vitest run` on the four files, 45 passed, 2026-11-13). Reading taken, not stated by the spec beyond "block-level page starts are the guaranteed minimum" (09 §7): "block-level" means offset 0 of the block (recorded in docs/adr/0306-lane-V-FE-097-112.md). Criterion 5 stays open (needs a compiled corpus and the page view).

**Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-18, second pass):** Criterion 5 stays open, nothing new ticked; the earlier reason is partly stale and partly not. Stale: EXP-055 and EXP-057 are built (`layout.json` with block and line starts, validated, written by the class's Lua code), so the file the engine adopts exists, and `adoptablePins` plus `layoutFlow` already adopt it (criterion 1). Still true: (1) no corpus document can be compiled to a PDF and a `layout.json` here. `packages/export-latex/src/exp-056-layout-pdf.compile.test.ts` is `it.skip` for `pages-20` because every multi-page corpus document holds Chinese, Japanese or Korean text and the sandbox image has no CJK face (EXP-026); a re-run of that file on 2026-11-18 reported 1 skipped, and a plain `podman run` of the test image on this machine failed with a cgroup error (`controller pids is not available`), although `sandboxReady()` reports the machine route as usable. A fixture written by hand as a `layout.json` would be compared with itself and would show nothing. (2) 'On-screen' page starts need the page view of FE-101, which is not mounted. (3) The tolerance still has no value or source: decision 22 of `docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md` is about the performance reference, not this, and the open question 6 in 02 §15 stands, so `PinOptions.tolerance` stays a required argument. Needs from others: a CJK face in the export image (EXP-026) or a corpus document without CJK text and more than one page, a working container run, the mount of FE-101, and the tolerance's value.

Audit (2026-11-13, evidence auditor): criterion 4 unticked. `adoptablePins` keeps page starts at any offset when the file says `block` (the test 'gives no line starts at block granularity' asserts all three pages, one of them B at offset 40), so mid-block page starts are used at block granularity, and no test shows that only block-level page starts are used; the line-start half is tested.

**Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-18):** Criterion 5 stays open; nothing new could be ticked. The export worker image (`localhost/docedit-export-worker:s4`) is present on this machine, so a corpus could be compiled, but three things stop an honest run. (1) No tolerance has a value: 02 §9.7 says "small" and the open question has no answer in docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md, so `PinOptions.tolerance` is a required argument; a corpus run with a tolerance chosen here would decide it silently, and a generous one would make the starts equal by construction (a kept pin starts its page where the typesetter did). (2) 'On-screen' means the page view, which the element does not mount (FE-101), and the editor's own lines come from the browser's measurer; equality of page starts against a PDF typeset with the house class's fonts is a measurement on the page view with the same fonts, which is not available as a test here. (3) The page-start overflow measure and the dropped-pin rule are tested on hand-built and generated trees (`reconcile-layout.test.ts`), not on the corpus. Needs from others: a tolerance from the owner or the spec, the page view (FE-101), and a corpus run that compiles each document and lays it out with the same faces.

### FE-101 Offer the editable page view as `view="print"`

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 3 | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 02 §7, §11; ADR 0005; 11 §6 · **Requirements:** R1 · **Depends on:** FE-065, FE-087, FE-095, FE-098, FE-099

As an author, I want to switch to an editable page view with margins, headers and footers in place, so that I can edit on pages.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given `view="print"` and the page view built, when the element renders, then editable pages with margins, headers and footers in place are shown.
- [x] Given the author switches between the web view and the page view, when the switch completes, then the editing core is the same, the selection is mapped and unconfirmed work is intact.
- [x] Given an unrecoverable error in the layout engine or the painter, when it occurs, then the element switches to the web view, keeps the document and unconfirmed work, and raises a non-fatal `de-error`.
- [x] Given a touch device, when `view="print"` is requested, then the web view is used (FE-113).
- [ ] Given fields and panes, when the page view is shown, then fields use the overlay (FE-093) and panes work as in the web view.

**Evidence (lane w1, 2026-11-18):** criteria 1 to 4. `packages/element/e2e/fe-101-page-view.spec.ts` in Chromium and WebKit (14 passed; the Firefox case passes in Firefox): `view="print"` on a desktop pointer shows the page view with the page setup's margins (96 CSS pixels from the page edge for 1440 twips) and the header story with its page number, hides the web surface and labels the view "Page layout"; typing, Enter and Backspace, arrow keys and Shift reach the one core through the bridge; a switch web to print to web keeps the same surface node, the text, the caret ("AB" then "Q" lands after it) and, after `flush()`, the confirmed content holds the work; with no canvas the first layout fails, the element shows the web view with the typed text kept and raises one non-fatal `de-error` (`internal`); Alt+Q opens the command search from the bridge and the Navigation pane opens beside the page view. Criterion 4: the same spec is the desktop control, and `e2e/fe-113-touch-view-choice.spec.ts` (touch emulation, `view="print"` gives the web view, Chromium) passes with the page view built. `packages/element/test/fe-101-page-view.test.ts` (14 tests, Node.js: the choice of view, the diff of a bridge change, key names, the break rule, the runs of a line) and `packages/engine/src/view/fe-098-page-input.test.ts` (8 tests: the core handle). Code: `packages/element/src/page-view/`, `packages/element/src/doc-editor.ts` (`#syncPageView`), `packages/engine/src/page-input.ts`, `packages/painter/src/painter.ts` (`PaintSource.runs`; `fe-101-runs.universal.test.ts`). Existing specs updated: `fe-065-view-print-fallback`, `fe-065-web-label`, `fe-106-web-view-available` and `embed-helpers.ts`.

**Evidence (lane U-FE-097-112, 2026-11-13; criterion 4 unticked by the reviewer, U4 group 2 round 2: the test passes with no touch logic in the element, because the element has one view and `selectView` is not called; it would pass on a desktop too, so it does not show that the touch decision is applied):** criterion 4 (not met), `packages/element/e2e/fe-113-touch-view-choice.spec.ts` (`view="print"` on a touch emulation shows the web view's editing surface and no page view), in Chromium (3 passed; Firefox and WebKit have no touch emulation in this run and are skipped). The page view is not built, so the outcome is the web view for every value (FE-113 Notes); criterion 1 (the page view with margins) and criterion 3 stay open.

**Notes:** U4 group 2 round 2 fix, 2026-11-13: criterion 4 stays open. The element now applies the touch decision (`selectView` from `doc-editor.ts`; FE-113 criterion 2 is ticked on `view="read"`, which shows the read view on a desktop and the web view on touch). For `view="print"` no test can tell the decision apart yet: `doc-editor.ts` passes `pageViewAvailable: false`, so `print` gives the web view on a desktop too. The criterion can be ticked when criterion 1 builds the page view and a desktop control shows the page view for `print`; then `packages/element/e2e/fe-113-touch-view-choice.spec.ts` (`view="print"` on touch) becomes the proof.

**Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-18):** Blocked; nothing new could be ticked. Decision 26 of docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md settles that the page view is the full editable one, but the editing surface of it does not exist. Criterion 1 needs editable pages in the element. The parts present are read-only: the painter's pages (`packages/painter/src/painter.ts`, `window.ts`), the overlay (FE-093) and the read view (`packages/element/src/read-view-element.ts`). Typing into painted pages needs the input bridge of FE-098, which is blocked on ADR 0380 (proposed, not accepted by the owner; see the FE-098 Notes), and a bridge built here would decide that ADR silently. Criterion 2 (switching with the same editing core, mapped selection and unconfirmed work) needs two editable views to switch between. Criterion 3 (fall back to the web view on a layout or painter error) needs a mounted page view to fail; a handler with nothing behind it would not show the behaviour. Criterion 4 stays as in the earlier Notes: `doc-editor.ts` still passes `pageViewAvailable: false` to `selectView`, so the touch decision cannot be told apart from the default until criterion 1 exists. Criterion 5 needs the page view to host panes and the overlay. Needs from others: the owner's decision on ADR 0380, FE-098 (the bridge), FE-099.

**Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-18, second pass):** Blocked again; nothing new could be ticked. Checked against the decisions file and the code of this branch. Applied: decision 26 (the full editable page view is to be built; C-WASM holds) and, by the run's reading of decisions 196 to 220, the recommended option of ADR 0380 (a textarea bridge on a window of the current block, movement and clicks from the layout tree, no EditContext). Neither makes the view exist. What is present: `toFlow` and `layoutFlow` (`packages/layout`), a canvas measurer (`packages/element/src/canvas-measure.ts`), the painter and its window (`paintTree`, `mountPageWindow`), the caret and selection geometry (`hitTest`, `caretGeometry`, `selectionRects` in `packages/layout/src/caret.ts`, `paintSelection`), the key logic (`page-keys.ts`) and the overlay (FE-093); the block offsets of the layout are CDM offsets, so a position maps to a ProseMirror position as the block's start plus one plus the offset. What is missing is the product code that joins them, and it is the sum of the unbuilt FE-098 and this story: (1) the bridge in the element (ADR 0380 item 1; only the S9 prototype exists, in `packages/perf/src/s9/`), with composition, marks on typed text, remote commits during composition, and sub-editors; (2) a width-of-span function for the caret and selection that follows runs, which needs the painter to draw runs: `PaintSource` gives one font family and one size for the whole document and there are no marks, so bold, italic, links, fields and headers and footers in their own styles would be drawn wrong; (3) a mount in the element: `doc-editor.ts` has no caller of the painter and passes `pageViewAvailable: false` (line 636), and the shell mounts its body through `core.mountBody`, which has no page-view alternative; (4) switching with the selection mapped and the unconfirmed work intact (criterion 2), and a fall-back on a layout or painter error with a non-fatal `de-error` (criterion 3), both of which need (3) first. Criterion 4 has its decision half tested (`packages/element/test/view-select.test.ts`: a touch device gets the web view for every `view` value with the page view available) and the element applies it (FE-113 criterion 2, on `view="read"`), but with `pageViewAvailable: false` no run can tell `print` on a touch device from `print` on a desktop, which is why the reviewer unticked it; it is left unticked on the same ground. Criterion 5 needs the page view to host the overlay and the panes. Building (1) to (3) as a thin stand-in here would decide ADR 0380's open input matrix and the painter's runs silently and would show 'editable pages' that draw the document in one face, so none was built. Needs from others: FE-098 (the bridge and its input-method matrix, which needs a person), the painter's runs, then this story's mount.

**Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-18, the mount):** the earlier "Blocked" note is superseded. `pageViewAvailable` was the constant `false` in `doc-editor.ts`; it is now whether the browser can measure for the page view (canvas, not Firefox; S5) and the page view has not failed. The page view is a lazy chunk over the same core, with ADR 0380's bridge; the design and what it leaves out are in docs/adr/0417-w1-page-view-mount.md (proposed). Criterion 5 stays open: panes work (Navigation opens beside the page view), but form fields are not on the overlay (FE-093): the painter places controls only for box fragments and a form field is an inline box in a line, so its control is not drawn; the field is painted as its stand-in text. Not built, and listed so they are not mistaken for done: tab stops and numbering-label tabs are painted with the default tab stop only; the caret on a justified line ignores the extra word spacing; no find highlight, no peers' carets, and a header or footer cannot be opened by clicking it in the page; the line break rule is a placeholder until FE-080; measurement is canvas on the page thread (the kernel is started but not read), so Firefox has no page view. A real device, real input methods and a screen reader have not run the page view. In this worktree the kernel's `dist` is older than its source (`abi_mismatch`), so `wasm-002` to `wasm-006` e2e specs fail here whatever the page view does; the page view does not depend on the kernel.

### FE-102 Keep the page view within its budgets

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 3 | M | must | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 11 §6; 13 §9; 00 §9; 02 §9.5 · **Requirements:** R1, R14 · **Depends on:** FE-088, FE-092, FE-101, FND-007, UX-021

As a maintainer, I want the page view's exit criteria measured on every release and the page view kept usable up to 1,000 pages, so that the budgets and the claim of 1,000 pages stay true.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given a 100-page document on the reference machine with CPU throttling, when typing is measured, then keystroke to paint is within 16 ms at the median and 50 ms at the 95th percentile (target).
- [ ] Given a 1,000-page document with virtualisation, when it is opened and scrolled, then it stays usable and only the visible pages and a small overscan are mounted.
- [ ] Given the thumbnails (UX-021), when they are scrolled in the 1,000-page document, then they are virtualised too.
- [ ] Given the 1,000-page document, when typing is measured, then the typing targets of UX-072 hold.
- [ ] Given a 100-page document, when memory is measured, then it stays under 300 MB.
- [ ] Given the phase 3 exit criteria, when a release is cut, then the S1 input matrix in the page view (FE-098), the screen-reader passes (FE-099) and the page-start comparison (FE-100) have all passed.

**Notes:** Phase 3 is gated on the evidence review at the end of phase 2. This story absorbed UX-075 during the cross-check.

**Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-18):** Blocked; nothing new could be ticked. Every criterion measures or exercises the editable page view in the element, which is not mounted (FE-101 Notes). Criteria 1 and 4 are typing measurements (keystroke to paint, the typing targets of UX-072) and need a bridge to type through (FE-098, blocked on ADR 0380). Criterion 2 (a 1,000-page document with only the visible pages and a small overscan mounted) and criterion 5 (memory of a 100-page document) need the page view in a browser; the painter's window (`packages/painter/src/window.ts`) virtualises read-only pages and is tested on its own (`window.universal.test.ts`), but that does not show the element's page view, and no memory figure may be quoted without a run of it on the reference machine (FND-007; ADR 0037). Criterion 3 needs the thumbnails (UX-021) in that view. Criterion 6 names FE-098, FE-099 and FE-100 as passed: FE-098 and FE-099 are blocked, and FE-100 criterion 5 is open. Needs from others: FE-101, FE-098, UX-021, the reference machine with CPU throttling.

**Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-18, second pass):** Blocked again; nothing new ticked. Checked against the decisions file and the branch after the merge. Decision 22 confirms the reference machine and laptop class of ADR 0037 (the tolerances stay provisional), and decision 93 keeps the one-update-per-frame scheduler and the UX-072 criterion; neither supplies a page view to measure. FE-101 is still blocked (the element mounts no painted pages and has no bridge, see its Notes), FE-098 is blocked on ADR 0380 being built and on a person's input-method matrix, and FE-088 and FE-089 still have their latency criteria open for the same reason. No figure is quoted: a keystroke-to-paint or memory number taken on anything else than the mounted page view on the reference machine would not be one of this story's. Criteria 2 and 3 are not shown by the painter's own window test (`packages/painter/src/window.universal.test.ts`), which mounts read-only pages in a bare harness. Needs from others: FE-101, FE-098, FE-099, FE-100 criterion 5, UX-021 and a run on the reference machine.

## Accessibility of the editing surface (02 §10)

**Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-18, first measurement):** nothing ticked. The page view exists now, so the budgets can be measured, and they are not met. `packages/element/src/fe-102-page-view-budget.universal.test.ts` (Chromium, Firefox, WebKit) builds a document of plain paragraphs and mounts the page view with the engine's `EditorCore`, canvas measurement, a synchronous frame and no CPU throttling, on the developer's Windows machine (not the reference machine of 11 section 6). Figures from Chromium, one run each: 98 pages, first layout and paint 0.8 s; 979 pages (22,500 paragraphs, an ad hoc run), 7.2 s; one keystroke from the bridge's `input` event to the painted page in the 98-page document, median 108 ms and 95th percentile 126 ms (20 samples); the same test in Firefox gave a median of 188 ms and in WebKit 207 ms in an earlier run. The target is 16 ms and 50 ms. Where the time goes (Chromium, 2,250 paragraphs, ad hoc timing): `toFlow` of the whole document about 27 ms, and `incrementalLayout.update` with every paragraph answered from its cache about 87 ms (the key of each paragraph is a `JSON.stringify` of its input, and the pagination, the shared-subtree pass and the freeze run over the whole tree). So a keystroke costs the document, not the block: the layout needs a dirty-block `toFlow` and a pagination that restarts at the first changed page (FE-089 sliced pagination exists and is not used by the page view). Virtualisation works: the test finds at most 5 pages in the DOM at 98 and at 300 pages and the last pages after a scroll to the end, in all three browsers. Criteria 2 to 5 need the reference machine and a 1,000-page document that opens in a usable time; criterion 3 needs the thumbnails (UX-021); criterion 6 needs FE-099 and FE-100.

### FE-103 Operate the editor from the keyboard and leave it again

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 02 §10; PRODUCT.md (accessibility); 13 §4 · **Requirements:** R11 · **Depends on:** FE-066, FE-021, UX-003, UX-013

As an author who uses only a keyboard, I want every function reachable by keyboard and a way out of the editing surface, so that Tab meaning indent never traps me.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a scripted set of tasks (open, type, format, comment, change a mode), when they are performed without a pointer, then every one can be completed.
- [x] Given focus is in the editing surface, where Tab means indent, next cell or next field, when Escape is pressed, then focus leaves the editing surface.
- [x] Given focus has left the editing surface by Escape, when Tab is pressed, then focus moves to the next control in page order, and Shift+Tab moves to the previous one.
- [x] Given any focused control, when it is looked at, then focus is visible, also under `forced-colors`.

**Evidence (lane U-FE-097-112, 2026-11-13):** criterion 2, `packages/ui/src/App.svelte` (the shell's key handler: Escape on the page, when no pane or menu holds it, blurs the focused element of the shell and is consumed, so the core does not see it) and `packages/ui/e2e/fe-103-keyboard.spec.ts` 'Escape leaves the editing surface', in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (`pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts e2e/fe-103-keyboard.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit --workers=1`: the Escape test passes in the three browsers). Regression: `e2e/toolbar.spec.ts`, `e2e/panes.spec.ts`, `e2e/shell.spec.ts` and `e2e/regions.spec.ts` in Chromium (56 passed, 1 failed: toolbar 'keeps the commands that do not fit reachable through "More"', which this change does not touch and which was not checked against the change before it).

**Evidence (lane V-FE-097-112, 2026-11-13):** criterion 4, `packages/ui/e2e/fe-103-forced-focus.spec.ts`: under `forced-colors: active` (Playwright `emulateMedia`), Tab moves from the page heading through the default shell's 7 focusable controls, and each focused control has an outline of at least 2 CSS pixels. Run with `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57061 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts e2e/fe-103-forced-focus.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit`: 3 passed (Chromium, Firefox, WebKit; the emulation was confirmed through `matchMedia` in each).

**Evidence (lane w1, 2026-11-17, criteria 1 and 3):** criterion 3: Escape now moves focus to an empty tab stop just after the surface (`.de-tab-origin`, `tabindex="-1"`, in `packages/ui/src/App.svelte`) instead of blurring, so every browser keeps its place for the next Tab. `packages/ui/e2e/fe-103-keyboard.spec.ts` ('after Escape, Tab moves to the next control in page order and Shift+Tab to the previous one') no longer skips WebKit; run with `$env:DOCEDIT_UI_PORT="57174"; pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts packages/ui/e2e/fe-103-keyboard.spec.ts packages/ui/e2e/fe-103-scripted-tasks.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit --retries=0` on Windows 11: 10 passed, 2 skipped (the forced-colours test in Firefox and WebKit, as before). The Escape test now expects focus on `.de-tab-origin`, not on no element. Criterion 1: `packages/ui/e2e/fe-103-scripted-tasks.spec.ts` on the shell harness (the real editing core and shell; the comment port is the harness's stand-in): Tab reaches the editing surface from the start of the page, the person types, selects the word with Shift+Arrow and makes it bold with Ctrl+B (`format.bold` becomes active), starts a comment through the command search (Alt+Q, 'New comment'), types it, reaches Post with Tab and posts it with Enter (one thread request), and changes the mode to Reviewing through the command search (`mode.review` becomes active). The page recorded no `pointerdown`, `pointerup`, `mousedown` or `mouseup`. Regression on the shell, toolbar, panes, regions, a11y and idref specs in Chromium: 68 passed, 1 failed (`panes.spec.ts` 'requests a pane's code only when it first opens' expects `view.comments` not to be registered, which the harness now registers; this change does not touch it and it was not checked against the code before it).

**Notes:** Moving between the shell landmarks with Ctrl+F6, and the alternative where a browser reserves it (shortcut table, 13 §4), are UX-023. Lane FE-092-104 (2026-11-13), no criterion ticked: criteria 1 to 4 are browser behaviour. `packages/ui/e2e/shell.spec.ts` already checks the focus order across the toolbar, the editing surface and the status bar, Tab leaving the component, and Escape (around its lines 81 to 105 and 170), but Playwright was not run in this lane, so that file is not evidence yet. Escape handling is in `packages/ui/src/App.svelte` and the toolbars' `onEscape`. No Node test covers them, and the forced-colours focus style was not checked. Lane U-FE-097-112 (2026-11-13): criterion 2 is ticked (Evidence). Escape is taken by the shell's key handler, so it is not the core's key; this is a reading of the spec, which does not say which layer takes it. Criterion 3 stays open: `fe-103-keyboard.spec.ts` 'after Escape, Tab moves to the next control in page order and Shift+Tab to the previous one' passes in Chromium and Firefox, but WebKit restarts Tab at the first control of the shell after the surface is blurred (the test is skipped there with that reason), so the criterion is not met in every browser. The fix would be a tab stop after the surface that takes the focus without a blur, which needs a change to the status bar's markup (a UX file, not this lane's). Criterion 4 stays open: the forced-colours test checks the outline of the first toolbar control in Chromium only, not every focused control. Criterion 1 (the scripted task set) is not run. Lane V-FE-097-112 (2026-11-13): criterion 4 is ticked (Evidence below). Criterion 1 is not run.

**Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-17):** every criterion is ticked. The five tasks are the ones the criterion names, run on the shell harness and in the three engines of the Playwright config; they are one scripted path, not every function of the editor, and not a run with a screen reader (FE-126). The mode task goes through the command search; the mode switcher control was not operated. The tab stop after the surface is a choice of this lane (no decision record): without it WebKit starts the next Tab at the first control after the surface loses focus.

### FE-104 Give every drag a keyboard or dialog alternative

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 02 §10; PRODUCT.md (accessibility) · **Requirements:** R11 · **Depends on:** FE-040, UX-003, UX-013

As an author who cannot drag, I want a keyboard or dialog way to do everything a drag does, so that no capability needs a pointer gesture.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given a table, when the user wants to change column widths or row heights, then a keyboard-operable dialog or command sets them with the same result as dragging.
- [ ] Given an image or figure, when the user wants to resize it, then a dialog or keyboard command sets its size with the same result as dragging.
- [ ] Given a block, when the user wants to move it, then keyboard commands move it up and down with the same result as dragging.
- [ ] Given the ruler's indent and tab-stop handles, when the user wants to move them, then a dialog or keyboard way does the same.
- [x] Given pointer-operated controls and handles, when their size is measured, then each is at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or satisfies the spacing exception of WCAG 2.2 criterion 2.5.8.
- [x] Given sticky bars (header, toolbar), when keyboard focus or the caret moves into the page, then it is never hidden behind a sticky bar.

**Evidence (lane V-FE-097-112, 2026-11-13), criterion 6, scope stated:** `packages/element/e2e/fe-104-target-size.spec.ts`, test 'no sticky or fixed element of the shell covers the editing surface': the shadow root of the S1 page has no element whose computed `position` is `sticky` or `fixed`, so the toolbar and the status bar take their place in the flow and cannot cover the caret. This is true of the default shell only; a bar added later must be tested again. Run with `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=57063 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts e2e/fe-104-target-size.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit`: passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit.

**Evidence (lane V-FE-097-112, 2026-11-13), criterion 5, partial:** the same file, test 'every visible pointer control of the default shell is at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels': every visible button, menu item, tab, checkbox, combo box, switch, option, input, select and link of the default shell (outside the document's own text) is at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels in its bounding box, in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit. The footnote reference in the text is left out as an inline target in a sentence (WCAG 2.2 criterion 2.5.8 exception; a reading, not a rule of this spec). The criterion is not ticked: the panes, dialogs and the context menu were not measured, and no handle exists yet (criteria 1 to 4 are not built).

**Evidence (lane U-FE-097-112, 2026-11-13), criterion 5, states beyond the default shell:** `packages/element/e2e/fe-104-target-size-states.spec.ts`: on the embed page with the element's shell, every visible pointer control in the shadow root is at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels in (a) the command search (Alt+Q), 18 controls measured, (b) the language dialog opened through it, 18 controls measured, and (c) the context menu (Shift+F10 on a selection), 20 controls measured (the counts are from the Chromium run, written to `packages/element/test-results/evidence/`). Run with `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=56061 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts e2e/fe-104-target-size-states.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit --workers=1 --retries=0`: 6 passed (two tests in each of Chromium, Firefox and WebKit; the lane reported 4, the reviewer's rerun gave 6). Criterion 5 stays unticked: the side panes (styles, outline, permissions and the others) are not measured, and the handles of criteria 1 to 4 are not built, so none is measured.

**Evidence (lane w1, 2026-11-17, criterion 5, panes, dialogs and the rest):** the panes and the remaining surfaces are now measured, and the one failure found is fixed. `packages/element/e2e/fe-104-target-size-panes.spec.ts` opens each pane the embed page offers through the command search and measures every visible pointer control in it (bounding box, at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels; the spacing exception is not used): Navigation (1 control), Recovered edits (1), Keyboard shortcuts (1), Permissions (2), Fields to fill (1), Sources (3), Roles (1) and Field properties (1) (counts from the Chromium run, `test-results/evidence/fe-104-target-size-panes.chromium.json`); the commands Comments, Styles, Accessibility checker, History, Find and Catch up are not offered by the embed page's backend (Find is the Navigation pane's search). `packages/ui/e2e/fe-104-target-size-panes.spec.ts` measures, on the shell harness, the Comments (5 controls), Styles (3), Checker (7) and History (5) panes, the comment (4), region (4) and export (8) dialogs, the mini toolbar (2) and the header and footnote panel. `packages/element/e2e/fe-070-math-editor.spec.ts` measures the buttons, the source field and the visual field of the math editor dialog. First run: the comments pane had three checkboxes of 14.7 by 14.7 pixels (`.de-check input`, 1.1em); `packages/ui/src/styles/shell.css` now sets them to at least 24 by 24. After the fix every control measured is at least 24 by 24: `$env:DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT="57175"; pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/fe-104-target-size-panes.spec.ts packages/element/e2e/fe-070-math-editor.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit --retries=0`: 9 passed; `$env:DOCEDIT_UI_PORT="57176"; pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts packages/ui/e2e/fe-104-target-size-panes.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit --retries=0`: passed in the three (Windows 11). Together with the earlier measurements of the default shell, the command search, the language dialog and the context menu, every surface of the shell that the harnesses reach is measured. Not measured: controls that an extension adds, and any control that appears only with more items than the harnesses hold. The handles of criteria 1 to 4 do not exist, so none is measured; the criterion's wording on handles is met only in that no handle is shipped.

**Notes:** Lane FE-092-104 (2026-11-13), no criterion ticked. The drag alternatives (criteria 1 to 4) are not built: `packages/engine/src/table.ts` has insert and delete row, column and table operations only (`TABLE_OPS`), with no column-width or row-height command, no image-size command, no block-move command, and no ruler indent or tab-stop command exists in `packages/ui`. Criteria 5 (24 by 24 CSS pixels, measured) and 6 (sticky bars) need a browser measurement, which was not run. Nothing here is invented as a measurement.

**Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-17):** criteria 1 to 4 are still not built and are not started in this lane: the engine has `resizeFigure` (FE-132) but nothing in the shell calls it (no size dialog, no figure selection in `CoreApi`); there is no command for the width of a table column or the height of a row, none to move a block up or down, and no ruler, so there is no indent or tab-stop handle to give an alternative to (and the shell has no drag at all, `packages/ui/test/ux-068-no-drag.test.ts`). Each is a command, a dialog or a shortcut, strings, a registry entry and tests, of about the size of FE-070; they are listed in the report as remaining.

### FE-105 Expose numbered paragraphs as lists to assistive technology

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 02 §10; 03 §4.3 · **Requirements:** R11 · **Depends on:** FE-066, CDM-042

As an author who uses a screen reader, I want numbered and bulleted paragraphs announced as list items, so that I know where I am in a list.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given three consecutive paragraphs with the same `listId`, when a screen reader reads them, then it announces list membership, the item's position and the list's size.
- [x] Given a nested numbering level, when a paragraph at that level is read, then its level is announced.
- [x] Given the model has no list nodes, when the editing surface is built, then list semantics are added through ARIA and axe reports no `aria-required-parent` or `aria-required-children` violation.
- [x] Given a list that continues across a region boundary or a table, when it is read, then its numbering and position follow the computed label.
- [x] Given a paragraph whose numbering changes by a remote edit, when the change arrives, then the exposed position updates.

**Evidence:** criteria 1 and 2, at the level of the ARIA attributes in the DOM: the web view applies `listSemantics` (`packages/engine/src/list-semantics.ts`, moved from the element, which re-exports it from `packages/element/src/list-semantics.ts`) as node decorations through `listSemanticsPlugin` (`packages/engine/src/view/list-view.ts`, registered in `packages/engine/src/view/web.ts`). `packages/element/e2e/list-semantics.spec.ts` reads the shadow root's DOM on the S1 page after numbering paragraphs through the body view: three consecutive items carry `role="listitem"`, `aria-level="1"` and `aria-posinset` 1 to 3 of `aria-setsize="3"`, and a nested item carries `aria-level="2"` (passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit on 2026-11-13, `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts e2e/list-semantics.spec.ts`). `packages/engine/test/list-view.test.ts` checks the decorations, that typing only maps them and that a numbering change recomputes them. The rules: `packages/element/test/list-semantics.test.ts` (a fast-check property over random lists and levels 0 to 8). Reviewed and unticked on 2026-11-13 because nothing applied the attributes; re-ticked with the DOM test. No screen reader was run: the manual passes are FE-126's. Criterion 5 (lane FE-105-117, 2026-11-13): `packages/engine/test/list-view-remote.test.ts` builds a remote rebuild with `rebuildTransaction` (`packages/engine/src/remote.ts`, the path a remote commit takes into the editor) that inserts a numbered paragraph before another item, and one that deletes an item; the exposed positions and sizes of the list follow (`pnpm vitest run packages/engine/test/list-view-remote.test.ts packages/engine/test/list-view.test.ts`: 7 passed). Criterion 4 (lane FE-105-117, round 4): `packages/engine/src/view/list-view.ts` now counts over the body in document order through regions, tables and cells, and skips paragraphs without numbering, so a list continues across them; `packages/engine/test/fe-105-continuation.test.ts` checks the positions against `computeLabels` (`@docedit/model`) for a list across a region, a table and a numbered cell, and in a fast-check property over 200 random layouts. Run on 2026-11-13 with `pnpm vitest run packages/engine/test/fe-105-continuation.test.ts packages/engine/test/list-view.test.ts packages/engine/test/list-view-remote.test.ts`: 13 passed; `packages/element/test/list-semantics.test.ts`: 5 passed. Criterion 4 unticked by the round 4 reviewer: the same test file records that an item of another list between two items (for example a numbered cell with its own `listId` inside a table) gives the later item `aria-posinset` 1 of 1 while its computed label is `2.`, so position does not follow the computed label there; the property test uses one list with `start` 1 and no `numberingRestart`, and numbering that comes from a paragraph style (`paragraphNumbering` in `computeLabels`) is not read by `list-view.ts`. Criterion 4 re-ticked (H group 2 fixer, round 4, 2026-11-13): `listDecorations` in `packages/engine/src/view/list-view.ts` now resolves each paragraph's numbering with `paragraphNumbering` and `styleTable` (`@docedit/model`, the style cascade with the table style, as `computeLabels` does), walks a footnote's paragraphs at the footnote's place (counted, not decorated), groups the items by `listId` and passes each list to `listSemantics` on its own; `listSemantics` (`packages/engine/src/list-semantics.ts`) takes an optional `restart` so that a `numberingRestart` starts a new set at its level. `packages/engine/test/fe-105-continuation.test.ts` checks against `computeLabels`: a numbered cell of another list inside the table gives the later item `aria-posinset` 2 of 2 (label `2.`), two interleaved lists across a region, a level that starts at 3 (labels `3.` to `5.`, positions 1 to 3), a `numberingRestart` inside a table cell, a restart at level 1 that leaves level 0 counted, numbering from a paragraph style, a footnote's numbered paragraph, and a fast-check property over 200 random layouts of two lists with random `start` (1 to 4) and restarts. Run on 2026-11-13 with `pnpm vitest run packages/engine/test/fe-105-continuation.test.ts packages/engine/test/list-view.test.ts packages/engine/test/list-view-remote.test.ts packages/element/test/list-semantics.test.ts`: 24 passed.

**Evidence (lane w1, 2026-11-17, criterion 3):** decision 91 of `docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md` (consecutive list paragraphs are drawn inside a real list element). `packages/engine/src/view/list-view.ts` adds, per run of consecutive items of one list (siblings with nothing between them), an empty `role="list"` widget before the first item with `aria-owns` naming every item of the run, and an `id` on each item (`de-li-<listId>-<index>`); `packages/engine/test/list-view.test.ts` (two new tests: one owner per run, a plain paragraph or a numbered heading ends a run; the existing attribute tests drop the new `id`). `packages/element/e2e/fe-105-axe-list-parent.spec.ts` no longer expects a failure: axe with `aria-required-parent` and `aria-required-children` reports nothing, and every item is named by an owner; 3 of 3 passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit on 2026-11-17 (`DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=57101 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts e2e/fe-105-axe-list-parent.spec.ts e2e/list-semantics.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit`: 9 of 9 passed). `pnpm exec vitest run --project node packages/engine`: 1207 passed.

**Notes:** Lane w1 (2026-11-17) reading of decision 91, to confirm: ProseMirror maps a node view's children one to one and the model has no list node, so no element can wrap sibling paragraphs. "Inside a real list element" is therefore met in the accessibility tree (an `aria-owns` owner placed just before the run, so order and place of the items are kept), not as DOM ancestry. This is the first candidate of ADR 0306 section 8. A real `<ul>` ancestor would need a wrapper node in the editing schema, which is a larger change; it is not done. No screen reader was run (FE-126). The older note follows. Manual screen-reader passes are part of FE-126. Criterion 3 stays open: the items have no `role="list"` parent, which ARIA requires of a `listitem`, so axe would report `aria-required-parent`. Lane V-FE-097-112 (round 3, 2026-11-13) measured it: `packages/element/e2e/fe-105-axe-list-parent.spec.ts` numbers three paragraphs on the S1 page and runs axe with `aria-required-parent` and `aria-required-children` in Chromium; it reports `aria-required-parent` on 3 nodes, as expected. The spec is an expected failure (`test.fail`) until a list parent is designed (ADR 0306, section 8, proposed; the choice between an `aria-owns` owner and a wrapper is not made). Criterion 3 stays unticked. How to give a run of paragraphs a list parent inside the editable surface (for example an `aria-owns` owner) is an open design point, not decided here; it also affects how fully criterion 1's "list membership" is announced. A numbered heading keeps its heading role and gets no list attributes, though it is counted in its list (a reading taken in `list-view.ts`, not stated by the spec). Criterion 4 was ticked in lane FE-105-117 round 4, unticked by the reviewer, and re-ticked by the round 4 fixer once interleaved lists, style-derived numbering, `start` and `numberingRestart` were counted and tested (see the Evidence). Reading taken by this package, not stated by the spec: a paragraph without numbering does not end a list, as the computed label does not end one, and an item of another list does not end it either: a list is counted by its `listId`, as the computed label counts it. Further readings, not stated by the spec, for the accessibility specialist to confirm: positions are ordinals from 1 whatever the level's `start`, as in an HTML `<ol start>` (the label shows the number); a `numberingRestart` starts a new ARIA set at its level, as a new `<ol>` would. Known gaps: a level whose `restartAfter` says "never restart" continues its label across parent items, while its ARIA set stays bounded by the parent item; a projection's hidden region whose counter effect (`counterEffects`) moves the label is not counted in the positions, which cover only the items the reader sees; a paragraph whose `numbering_def` is missing gets no label but is still exposed as an item. Criterion 5 is ticked (lane FE-105-117): the plugin recomputes on any change that may move list items, and the remote rebuild test covers an insertion and a deletion; a remote change to `numbering` alone is not tested. Reading taken by this package, not stated by the spec: a sub-list sits inside its parent item's segment, so top-level siblings are counted across it. Lane w1 (2026-11-17), no criterion ticked: criterion 3 is blocked on a design choice that is not this lane's to make. `packages/element/e2e/fe-105-axe-list-parent.spec.ts` is still an expected failure (`test.fail`) and is not changed. ADR 0306 section 8 names the two candidates, an `aria-owns` owner outside the paragraphs and a wrapper, and says each changes the accessibility tree that the screen-reader passes of FE-126 will judge. An `aria-owns` owner would move the items, in the tree, out of the contenteditable surface (whose role makes its children presentational) into the owner's place, so the reading order of the text could change; a wrapper has no place in a ProseMirror surface without a list node in the model. The owner's decisions of 2026-11-15 do not list it, so it is still open. Nothing was built and nothing was probed with a screen reader. Once the choice is made, the work is: the attributes in `packages/engine/src/view/list-view.ts`, then removing `test.fail` from the spec.

### FE-106 Reflow the web view for people who zoom

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | S | must | Author |

**Spec:** 02 §10; PRODUCT.md (accessibility) · **Requirements:** R11 · **Depends on:** FE-066

As an author who zooms in, I want the text to reflow to the window, so that I never scroll sideways to read a line.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a viewport equivalent to 400 percent zoom of a 1280 CSS pixel window (320 CSS pixels wide), when the web view shows a document, then paragraphs reflow with no horizontal scrolling and no clipped text (WCAG 2.2 criterion 1.4.10).
- [x] Given tables and images wider than the viewport, when they are shown, then they are the only content that may scroll horizontally, within their own containers.
- [x] Given the page view cannot meet the reflow criterion with fixed-width pages, when a user needs to zoom, then the web view remains available (FE-101).
- [x] Given the browser's text-only zoom or a larger default font size, when the web view renders, then no text is clipped or overlapped.

**Evidence (lane U-FE-097-112, 2026-11-13):** criterion 1, `packages/element/e2e/fe-106-reflow.spec.ts`, in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (`pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts e2e/fe-106-reflow.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit --workers=1`: 9 passed). The test opens the S1 page at a 320 CSS pixel viewport, types a 120-character unbroken word into a paragraph through the keyboard, and checks that the page and the editing surface do not scroll sideways and that no paragraph extends past the viewport's right edge.

**Evidence (lane V-FE-097-112, 2026-11-13), criterion 2:** `packages/element/e2e/fe-106-wide-content.spec.ts`. The model gives table cells and images no width (`packages/engine/src/schema.ts`), so the wide content is a stand-in: a style injected into the shadow root gives a cell a minimum inline size of 600 CSS pixels, and gives the image span an inline size of 600 CSS pixels. The table scrolls inside its own box (`overflow-x: auto`), and the page, the surface and every other element stay within the 320 pixel viewport. The image widened the surface before the rule `.de-image` (`display: inline-block; max-inline-size: 100%`) was added to `packages/element/src/styles/reflow.css`; with it the image is held to its paragraph's width. Run with `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=57062 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts e2e/fe-106-wide-content.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit`: passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (Firefox was run with `--workers=1`; with four workers its two table tests failed once on trace-file errors in the shared output folder and passed on rerun). `packages/element/test/fe-106-reflow.test.ts` and `packages/element/test/styles.test.ts` (vitest): 13 passed. Criterion 4 stays open: the doubled font size is still not a browser text-only zoom.

**Evidence (lane w1, 2026-11-18), criteria 3 and 4:** Criterion 3: `packages/element/e2e/fe-106-web-view-available.spec.ts` (320 CSS pixel viewport, `view="print"`: the web view is shown, labelled, with no paragraph past the edge and no sideways overflow; then `read` and back to `web` ends in the web view again) and the property tests 'the web view stays available to people who zoom' in `packages/element/test/view-select.test.ts` (`view="web"` gives the web view whatever the page offers; the page view is shown only when asked for, built and not on touch). Run on this machine on 2026-11-18 with `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts e2e/fe-106-web-view-available.spec.ts e2e/fe-106-reflow.spec.ts e2e/fe-106-wide-content.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit --workers=1 --retries=0`: 18 passed; `pnpm exec vitest run --project node packages/element/test/view-select.test.ts`: 10 passed. Criterion 4: `packages/element/e2e/fe-106-text-zoom.spec.ts`. Each test opens the S1 page in its own browser profile with the default font size doubled to 32 CSS pixels (Chromium: `webkit.webprefs.default_font_size` in the profile's Preferences file, run in Playwright's new headless mode, `channel: 'chromium'`; Firefox: `font.size.variable.x-western` in `firefoxUserPrefs`), checks that a probe in `font-size: medium` is at least 32 pixels (so a browser that ignored the profile fails), and audits every text box in the editor's shadow root at 1280 and at 320 CSS pixels wide: no ancestor with `overflow: hidden` or `clip` cuts a text box, no two text boxes overlap by more than 30 percent of the smaller height, and no element or text of the editor reaches past the viewport or scrolls sideways. Run with `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts e2e/fe-106-text-zoom.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=firefox --workers=1 --retries=0`: 4 passed (Chromium and Firefox, two widths). The first run at 320 pixels failed: the shell's header, mode button and status-bar zoom group did not wrap and reached 673 pixels; `.de-header`, `.de-header-actions` and `.de-zoom` now have `flex-wrap: wrap` (`packages/ui/src/styles/shell.css`), and the test passes with it.

**Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-18):** All four criteria are ticked. Limits. Criterion 3: the page view (FE-101) is not built, so the web view is the only editing view; the test shows that it is shown and reflows for `view="print"` at 320 pixels and that every other value can return to it, and it will keep that check when the page view is built. Criterion 4: the setting tested is a larger default font size, which Playwright can set in a profile; the browser's text-only zoom menu is browser chrome that Playwright cannot drive, and it was not run. WebKit has no default font size setting Playwright can reach, so criterion 4 was run in Chromium and Firefox only, and Edge not at all. The audit measures text boxes with `Range.getClientRects`; it does not see text clipped by a `clip-path` or drawn over by an element with no text. The test page's own heading (outside the editor) is wider than 320 pixels at the doubled font size and is not the editor's.

**Notes:** Lane FE-105-117, round 2 (2026-11-13), no criterion ticked. The reflow rules are in `packages/element/src/styles/reflow.css` (the editing surface and sub-editors shrink to the container and break long words; images are capped at the width; a table is a block with its own horizontal scroll), concatenated into the shared sheet by `packages/element/src/styles.ts`. `packages/element/test/fe-106-reflow.test.ts` (7 passed with `pnpm vitest run packages/element/test/fe-106-reflow.test.ts packages/element/test/styles.test.ts`) guards the rules as CSS text and the concatenation order; it is not a layout measurement. Criteria 1, 2 and 4 need a browser at 320 CSS pixels with a table and an image wider than the viewport, and a larger font: not run here (no browser run in this lane). Criterion 3 stays open: `packages/element/src/view-select.ts` falls back to the web view for `view="print"`, but the element does not mount views yet (`doc-editor.ts` keeps `view` on the attribute; FE-065 Notes). The web view's own page-width rules in `packages/ui/src/styles/shell.css` were read and not changed: the shell belongs to UX. Lane U-FE-097-112 (2026-11-13): `fe-106-reflow.spec.ts` also runs a six-cell table and a doubled host font size at 320 CSS pixels; both pass in the three browsers and do not widen the page. Criterion 2 stays open: the table fits its own box (its cells wrap, `overflow-wrap: anywhere`), so no table wider than the viewport is shown, and the image rule targets `img`, while the model's image node renders a `div`, so the rule for images is not exercised. Criterion 4 stays open: the doubled font size is a host style, not the browser's text-only zoom, and the test checks sideways scroll and the right edge only, not clipping or overlap. Criterion 3 stays open (FE-101). Lane FE-105-117, round 6 (no criterion ticked): the image node renders `span.de-image` with `role="img"` (`packages/engine/src/schema.ts`), so the `img` rule above does not reach it. A rule for the span would need `display` changed from inline, which changes the layout of images in paragraphs; not made without a browser check at 320 CSS pixels (ADR 0208).

### FE-107 Announce saving, rejected edits and collaborators

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 02 §10; PRODUCT.md (accessibility) · **Requirements:** R11, R7 · **Depends on:** FE-007, FE-010, FE-036, FE-037, PRM-024, PRM-028, UX-016, UX-026, UX-027

As an author who uses a screen reader, I want changes of save state, rejected edits and collaborators arriving or leaving announced, so that I know what is happening without watching the screen.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the save state changes, when it changes to saving, saved, unsaved or read-only (UX-016), then a live region inside the shadow root announces the new state once.
- [x] Given an edit is refused by the guard or rejected by the server, when it happens, then a live region announces that it was not applied and where it happened, with the reason PRM provides.
- [x] Given a collaborator arrives or leaves, when presence changes, then a live region announces it with the name resolved through `resolveUsers` (UX-025).
- [x] Given content is set aside as recovered edits (FE-010), when it happens, then a live region announces it with the recovered edits count (UX-017).
- [x] Given several announcements occur within a short time, when they are made, then none is lost.
- [x] Given the announcement text, when it is produced, then it comes from external interface strings (UX-064) in the current locale, and the live regions are in the same root as the content they describe.
- [x] Given typing, when keystrokes are made, then nothing is announced for them.

**Evidence:** Fixer round 2 (B group 1, 2026-11-13): criterion 5 is ticked again in a browser. `packages/element/src/live-region.ts` (`mountLiveRegion`: one `role="status"` region, `aria-live="polite"`, `aria-atomic="true"`, first in the shadow root), mounted by `packages/element/src/doc-editor.ts`, which starts an `Announcer` per session with the region as its sink, feeds it each `de-status` save state, and removes the region on `destroy()`. `packages/element/e2e/announcements.spec.ts`, run in Chromium on 2026-11-13 with `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts announcements element.spec --project=chromium` (40 passed, `element.spec.ts` included): 'a burst of notices in one task reaches the region in full and in order' (six notices of all four kinds given in one task; a mutation observer on the region sees all six sentences, in order), 'typing changes the save state, and the change reaches the region' and 'the region is polite, atomic and inside the shadow root'. Earlier review text: criterion 5 was unticked by review (2026-11-13): `Announcer` is imported by nothing but its own test and is not exported from the package, so the product announces nothing yet and no announcement can be lost or kept; the queue test becomes evidence once a live region and the notices are wired. Criterion 5, at the level of the announcer's queue: `packages/element/src/announcer.ts` (`Announcer` holds every accepted sentence until the sink takes it, one per scheduled turn, in the order accepted) and `packages/element/test/announcer.test.ts` (a burst is spread over three turns; a fast-check property over 200 random sequences of save-state, rejected, recovered and presence notices checks that each accepted notice is delivered once, in order). Run on 2026-11-13 with `pnpm vitest run packages/element/test/announcer.test.ts`: 9 passed. The sentences of criteria 1 to 4 are produced there too (`ANNOUNCE_EN`, the save-state change announced once, the refused edit with its reason, presence named through `resolveUsers`, recovered edits by count), but no live region is written yet, so those criteria stay open. Criterion 3, presence (lane FE-105-117, 2026-11-13, not ticked): `packages/element/src/presence-notices.ts` (`presenceChanges`, `PresenceWatch`: a session is the unit; see the fixer round 5 text below for the baseline) is fed by `packages/element/src/session.ts` from the editor's `presence` view and calls the new `onPresence` option, which `packages/element/src/doc-editor.ts` routes into the announcer as a `presence` notice (`packages/element/src/editor.ts` exposes `core.presence` on the handle). `packages/element/test/fe-107-presence.test.ts` checks the diff, a fast-check property over 200 random snapshot sequences, and that the session reports the editor's changes and not the baseline: 30 passed with `pnpm vitest run packages/element/test/fe-107-presence.test.ts packages/element/test/session.test.ts packages/element/test/announcer.test.ts`; `tsc --noEmit` is clean for `packages/element` and `packages/element/tsconfig.test.json`. The live region's write of a name is not run in a browser here, so criterion 3 stays open. Fixer round 5 (H group 2, 2026-11-13): the session wiring test above used a stand-in editor whose list already held the peers; in the product the core's list is empty until the first `presence.state` arrives, after the session starts watching, so every peer of a busy document was announced. Now `packages/element/src/session.ts` hands the core a wrapped presence port and takes the core's list after its first `presence.state` as the baseline, or the list at the end of an opening window (`DEFAULT_PRESENCE_BASELINE_MS`, 500 ms, a provisional default) when none comes; `PresenceWatch` starts without a baseline until `settle()`.
