ADR 0208 records the reading and its limits (G42: the protocol does not mark a list on joining). `packages/element/test/fe-107-presence-core.test.ts` runs a Session with the headless core against backend-mock: two peers already there are not announced (the service's list on joining is added to the reader's port, since backend-mock sends none), a later arrival and a departure are; a reader who opened alone hears the first arrival; nothing is reported after close. Run on 2026-11-13 with `pnpm vitest run packages/element/test/fe-107-presence.test.ts packages/element/test/fe-107-presence-core.test.ts packages/element/test/session.test.ts packages/element/test/announcer.test.ts`: 34 passed; `tsc --noEmit` clean for `packages/element` and `packages/element/tsconfig.test.json`. Criterion 3 stays open (no browser run of the live region).

**Evidence (lane V-FE-097-112, 2026-11-13):** criterion 3, `packages/element/e2e/fe-107-presence-live.spec.ts`: a reader's `<doc-editor>` with `resolveUsers` set before it connects, and a second editor of another user on the same document. The reader's live region says `Bob Example joined the document.` once when the second editor connects, and `Bob Example left the document.` once when it is destroyed and removed (the name is the one `resolveUsers` returned, not the identifier). Run with `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts e2e/fe-107-presence-live.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit`: 3 passed (Chromium, Firefox, WebKit). The test waits 1.5 s after the reader mounts, so that the peer is a change and not part of the reader's first list; without that wait the Chromium run did not announce the arrival. Criteria 1, 2, 4 and 6 are not checked here.

**Notes:** Element-2 round 2 ticks criterion 5 only. Not met yet: the live region inside the shadow root and its wiring (no `aria-live` element is mounted by `doc-editor.ts`; the sink is injected); presence events and the guard or server rejection are not raised by the element; the save-state, recovered-edit wiring to `de-status` and `de-recovered-edits` is not made (kept out of `doc-editor.ts`, which other agents edit). Criterion 7 is not met: the announcer has no keystroke input, but `saved` after each keystroke would still be announced once per save-state change, so the transitions that are announced are a decision for the accessibility specialist, and until it is made the save-state sentence after every keystroke is a flood risk. Criterion 6 is not met: the sentences come from `ANNOUNCE_EN` (a table per locale, in `announcer.ts`, not yet fed by UX-064); the live region is now in the root. Politeness: the region is `aria-live="polite"` for every announcement (`live-region.ts`); the spec does not state a level (open question 26), and whether a rejected edit should be assertive is the accessibility specialist's decision. Fixer round 2: only the save state of `de-status` is fed to the element's announcer. Refusals and recovered edits are already announced by the shell's own live region (`packages/ui/src/App.svelte`, `NoticeTracker`), so feeding `de-recovered-edits` here too would announce them twice; which region owns them is left to the accessibility specialist. Criterion 1 is not re-ticked in the fixer round (it may only re-tick what the review unticked), though 'typing changes the save state, and the change reaches the region' runs that path. Lane FE-105-117 (2026-11-13) checked the wiring, which is newer than the text above: `doc-editor.ts` mounts the live region in the root (`mountLiveRegion`, `packages/element/src/live-region.ts`), starts an `Announcer` per session with the live region as sink, and feeds each `de-status` change to it once (`#emit`). Criterion 1 is still unticked: no test runs that path (the element's DOM test needs a browser, not run here); the announcer's queue test does not cover it.

**Evidence (lane w1, 2026-11-17):** criteria 1, 2, 4, 6 and 7. Source: `packages/element/src/announcer.ts` (`settleMs`, `DEFAULT_SETTLE_MS` of 1000 ms, `translate`, `ANNOUNCE_KEYS`), `packages/element/src/doc-editor.ts` (passes both), `packages/element/src/editor.ts` (`MountedEditor.text`), `packages/ui/src/i18n/en-GB.ts` (`announce.sync.*`, `announce.presence.*`, `announce.edit.rejected`, `announce.recovered.count`), `packages/ui/package.json` (export `./strings`). Node tests: `packages/element/test/fe-107-settle.test.ts` (a save state is said once after it holds; a run that ends where it began says nothing), `packages/element/test/fe-107-catalogue.test.ts` (every sentence is in the catalogue; host replacement strings, a pseudo-locale and the fallback table). Run with `pnpm exec vitest run --project node packages/element/test/fe-107-catalogue.test.ts packages/element/test/fe-107-settle.test.ts packages/element/test/announcer.test.ts packages/element/test/fe-107-presence.test.ts packages/element/test/fe-107-presence-core.test.ts packages/element/test/session.test.ts`: 47 passed. Browser: `packages/element/e2e/fe-107-live-regions.spec.ts` (read-only reader hears "This document is read-only."; a guard refusal is said by the shell's region in the same root, without the scope name or the refused character; a held commit is said once as saving then once as saved; typing 13 characters says nothing for a run that ends saved; a service refusal is said as set aside, with the count; a host string and the locale reach the sentence), with `announcements.spec.ts` and `fe-107-presence-live.spec.ts`. Run on 2026-11-17 in Chromium on this machine with `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts fe-107 announcements --project=chromium`: 9 passed. Firefox and WebKit were not run for the new spec. Decisions are proposed in ADR 0376 (settle period, which region owns which sentence, "where" as a kind of place).

**Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-17):** Criterion 7 is met by the settle period, which is a provisional default (ADR 0376) the accessibility specialist should set; politeness stays polite (open question 26). Criterion 2 is met by two regions: the shell's catalogue says refusals and rejections (`notice.refused.*`, `notice.rejected.*`), not the element's announcer, so a reader hears each once.

**Notes (earlier):** Which transitions are announced (every "saving" would flood a screen reader) is a design decision for an accessibility specialist; the spec only says that saving, rejected edits and arrivals and departures are announced. The politeness level of each announcement is not specified (Open questions). This story absorbed UX-024 during the cross-check.

### FE-108 Keep ARIA references inside the shadow root

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1b | S | must | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 02 §10 · **Requirements:** R11 · **Depends on:** FE-013

As a maintainer, I want every labelled control and its label in the same root, so that ARIA references never break at the shadow boundary.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given every `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-controls`, `aria-owns`, `aria-activedescendant` and `for` reference in the editor, when a test resolves it, then the target is in the same shadow root.
- [x] Given a lint rule, when a component references an element outside its root, then the build fails.
- [ ] Given field controls and their labels (12 §11) and overlay controls (FE-093), when they are checked, then label and control share a root.
- [x] Given the check, when axe runs, then it finds no unresolved reference.

**Evidence (lane U-FE-097-112, 2026-11-13):** criterion 4. `packages/element/e2e/fe-108-axe-idrefs.spec.ts` now also fails on `results.incomplete` for `aria-valid-attr-value` and `aria-valid-attr` (the "needs review" result axe gives an unresolved ID inside a shadow tree), on the default editor and on the editor with the full toolbar. Run with `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=56060 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts e2e/fe-108-axe-idrefs.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit --workers=1 --retries=0`: 6 passed (Chromium, Firefox, WebKit). The port is set because the default port 4791 served another run's build and the page did not load there. Scope: the two states the spec mounts; overlays and panes are not in it (criterion 1 stays open).

**Evidence (lane V-FE-097-112, 2026-11-13):** criterion 4, `packages/element/e2e/fe-108-axe-idrefs.spec.ts`: axe, with its `aria-valid-attr-value` and `aria-valid-attr` rules run through the shadow root, reports no violation on the default editor and on the editor with the toolbar and the status bar, and the rule passed on the references of each state. Run with `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts e2e/fe-108-axe-idrefs.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit`: 6 passed (Chromium, Firefox, WebKit). Only these two states are checked; the overlays, panes and dialogs (criteria 1 and 3) are not. Unticked by the reviewer (round 1): axe reports an `aria-labelledby` or `aria-describedby` whose ID is not found as "needs review" (`incomplete`), not as a violation (axe-core 4.14.0, `ariaValidAttrValueEvaluate`, message `noIdShadow` inside a shadow tree). The spec reads `results.violations` only, so it passes with a broken reference: a reviewer's probe set `aria-labelledby="does-not-exist"` in the shadow root and got 0 violations and 1 `aria-valid-attr-value` incomplete. The spec must also fail on `results.incomplete` for these rules before the criterion can be ticked.

**Evidence (lane V-FE-097-112, fixer round 1, 2026-11-13), criterion 4:** `packages/element/e2e/fe-108-axe-idrefs.spec.ts` now fails on any node that `aria-valid-attr-value` or `aria-valid-attr` lists under `results.incomplete` as well as `results.violations` (that edit was already in the file when this round started). `packages/element/e2e/fe-108-axe-idrefs-control.spec.ts` (new) proves the check can fail: with `aria-labelledby="fe-108-does-not-exist"` set on a toolbar button in the shadow root, axe lists exactly that node under `aria-valid-attr-value`, and a broken `aria-describedby` on the editing surface is listed too; the same file runs the check on a third state, the regions document with the full toolbar, and finds nothing. Run with `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=57371 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts e2e/fe-108-axe-idrefs.spec.ts e2e/fe-108-axe-idrefs-control.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit --retries=0`: 15 passed. Coverage caveat: three states only (default editor; toolbar and status bar; regions document with the toolbar). The overlays, panes and dialogs (criteria 1 and 3) are not opened, so the criterion is met for the states reached and must be rerun when they are built.

**Evidence (lane w1, 2026-11-15), criteria 1 and 2, and the field half of 3:** Criterion 2: `packages/ui/test/support/idref-lint.ts` is the rule (every ID a component references must be an ID the same component file gives to one of its own elements, so the reference cannot leave the component's root) and `packages/ui/test/fe-108-idref-lint.test.ts` runs it (Node.js, 9 of 9 tests): fixtures with a reference to an ID from a property, to a literal no element carries, and with one foreign side of a condition all fail; every `.svelte` file of `packages/ui/src` passes; no source of the shell, the element, the core or the painter sets such a reference from script. The test is part of `pnpm test`, so of `pnpm check`. Criterion 1: `packages/ui/e2e/fe-108-idrefs-all-surfaces.spec.ts`, in the shell's harness, which mounts the shell in a shadow root as the element does. A recorder reads every reference in the root after every change and resolves each ID with `ShadowRoot.getElementById`. Surfaces opened: every pane the harness offers (outline, comments, catch-up, find, styles, recovered edits, checker, shortcuts, permissions with a region opened and a principal searched, history, fill summary), the language, region, comment and export dialogs, the context menu, command search with options, the toolbar's overflow menu, the mode switcher, the people list, and each tab of the full toolbar; a control test shows the recorder reports a reference to an element outside the root. Criterion 3, field half: the same file's test of the field properties panel (ten labels or more, each to a form control of the panel). Run on the lane's Windows 11 machine with `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57103 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts packages/ui/e2e/fe-108-idrefs-all-surfaces.spec.ts --retries=0`: 12 passed (4 tests in each of Chromium, Firefox and WebKit). The element-level checks of the earlier lanes (`packages/element/e2e/fe-108-aria-refs.spec.ts`, `fe-108-axe-idrefs*.spec.ts`) stand as recorded above.

**Notes:** Lane w1 (2026-11-15): criteria 1 and 2 ticked, criterion 3 stays open. Criterion 2: the rule is a source check run by the test runner, not an ESLint rule, because ESLint does not parse `.svelte` files in this workspace (that needs `eslint-plugin-svelte` and a root configuration change). It is stricter than the criterion: a reference to an element of another component in the same root fails too; none exists today. It reads markup with a small scanner, not Svelte's parser; an expression it cannot reduce fails the rule, it never passes silently. Criterion 1 rests on both tests: the rule covers every reference written in a component; the browser run resolves the references of every surface it opens. Not shown in the browser run, and so covered by the rule only: messages that appear only on an error (the language, region and export dialogs), the mention list of the comment box, the assignee filter of the comments pane, the reason of a disabled context-menu item, and the panes the harness does not offer (assistant, PDF, review, fields). If the reviewer wants each of these opened in a browser, criterion 1 should be unticked until then. Criterion 3 stays open: the properties panel of a field is checked, but controls placed on a page by the painter's overlay (FE-093) get whatever element a caller's factory returns, and the editor does not build field controls with labels there yet, so there is nothing to check. The properties panel uses fixed IDs (`fp-key` and so on): two panels in one root would clash; not changed here. Earlier: Lane U-FE-097-112 (2026-11-13), no criterion ticked. `packages/element/e2e/fe-108-aria-refs.spec.ts` runs in a browser on the embed page and passes in Chromium (`pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts e2e/fe-108-aria-refs.spec.ts --project=chromium`, 2 passed): every `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-controls`, `aria-owns`, `aria-activedescendant` and `for` reference in the element's shadow root resolves through `getElementById` on that root. It reaches two states only: the default editor (2 references) and the editor with the full toolbar and the status bar (8 references). Criterion 1 needs every reference in the editor, including the overlays and panes (FE-020, FE-093), which this test does not open, so it stays open. Criterion 2 (a lint rule) is a root configuration change, not made here. Criterion 4 needs axe run on the shadow root; `@axe-core/playwright` is a dependency of `packages/ui` only, not of `packages/element`, and adding it is a `package.json` change this lane may not make, so it stays open. Criterion 3 (field controls and overlay controls) is not reached, as the overlay is not built.

**Notes (earlier):** Lane FE-105-117 (2026-11-13), no criterion ticked. `packages/element/test/shadow-root-refs.test.ts` is a Node.js source guard: no `document.getElementById`, `getElementsBy*` or `querySelector*` in `packages/ui/src`, `packages/element/src` (the PDF viewer excluded, it runs as its own page) and `packages/engine/src`. It is a proxy for criterion 2, not the lint rule (the lint rule is a root configuration change). Criteria 1, 3 and 4 need a DOM or a browser run, not run here; the same-root property of each reference is not tested at run time. Reads of `document.body` (features/math.ts) are diagnostics, not lookups, and are not matched.

## Internationalisation and reach (02 §11, §12)

### FE-111 Edit right-to-left and complex-script content in the web view

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 4 | L | should | Author |

**Spec:** 02 §11; 11 §7; 03 §4.1 (`dir`) · **Requirements:** R1, R12 · **Depends on:** FE-066, FE-123

As an author who writes in Arabic, Hebrew or an Indic script, I want to edit in my script in the web view, so that I can use the editor in my language.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given `settings.dir` and a paragraph's `dir`, when the web view renders, then the paragraph is rendered with that direction.
- [x] Given mixed left-to-right and right-to-left text, when it is typed, selected and edited, then display order, caret movement and selection follow the browser's bidirectional handling in each supported browser.
- [ ] Given a corpus in Arabic, Hebrew, Devanagari and Thai, when the input matrix of FE-123 is extended to it, then typing and composition pass.
- [x] Given the region guard, when the caret and selection are used in right-to-left text, then clamping at region boundaries behaves as for left-to-right text.
- [ ] Given a document with right-to-left content, when it is exported, then the PDF shows the same direction and script shaping (checked with EXP).

**Evidence (lane U-FE-097-112, 2026-11-13):** criterion 1, `packages/element/e2e/fe-111-direction.spec.ts` on the demo document `rtlDocument()` (`packages/element/demo/embed-page.ts`: `settings.dir` `rtl`; paragraphs with `dir` `rtl`, `ltr` and none), in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (`pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts e2e/fe-111-direction.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit`: 6 passed). The test reads the surface's and each paragraph's `dir` and computed direction, and checks that right-to-left text ends at the surface's right edge. Criterion 2 (lane U-FE-097-112, 2026-11-13, not ticked): `packages/element/e2e/fe-111-bidi-caret.spec.ts` types mixed text into the right-to-left paragraph (the logical text is what was typed) and grows a selection with Shift+Right; the selected text at each step equals that of a plain contenteditable element with the same direction and text, in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (3 passed). Not ticked because the display order and the caret movement by visual rectangles are not measured, only the logical text and the selected text.

**Evidence (lane w1, 2026-11-18):** criteria 2 and 4 ticked, run on Windows with Playwright 1.65 (Chromium, Firefox and WebKit builds installed on this machine), `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts <files> --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit --retries=0` on demo port 4871. Criterion 2: `packages/element/e2e/fe-111-visual-caret.spec.ts` now runs in WebKit as well (it reads the real range of the shadow root with `getComposedRanges`, where `getSelection` is retargeted to the host) and draws the caret where a plain contenteditable element draws it after each ArrowRight; `packages/element/e2e/fe-111-bidi-caret.spec.ts` (typing, Shift+Arrow selection against the reference, and a new test that moves the caret, deletes both ways and types in the middle of mixed text and ends with the text the reference gives). Together they cover typed, selected, edited text, the caret's drawn position (display order) and the selection in the three engines; the reference is the browser's own bidirectional handling, so no order of our own is asserted. The two files (3 tests) passed 45 of 45 in a repeated run (5 repeats in 3 browsers). Edge uses the Chromium engine and was not run separately. Both files now press End after the click so that the caret starts at the logical end: in Firefox the click alone sometimes put it at the start (1 failure in about 25 runs before the change, none in 45 after). Criterion 4: `packages/element/e2e/fe-111-rtl-regions.spec.ts` (15 passed) on a right-to-left document with a region (`rtl-regions`, `rtlRegionDocument()` in `packages/element/demo/embed-page.ts`): Shift+arrows from two characters inside the region's end select exactly the last two characters and no further, in the text direction (ArrowLeft) and then down a line; the same at the region's start (ArrowRight, then up); a selection inside the region and across its two paragraphs is kept; Backspace at the start and Delete at the end change nothing; a drag from the region to the paragraph after it stays inside the region. These are the cases of `e2e/regions.spec.ts` (FE-038) with the keys of right-to-left text.

**Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-18):** Partial: criteria 1, 2 and 4 ticked. Criterion 3 stays open: the matrix of FE-123 covers Japanese, Chinese and Korean compositions, and extending it to Arabic, Hebrew, Devanagari and Thai means composition on real keyboards and input methods (Devanagari and Thai in particular), which Playwright cannot drive and this machine cannot run by hand; `keyboard.type` of those scripts would test typing only, and the criterion says "typing and composition pass". FE-123's own file is another story's and was not edited. Criterion 5 needs EXP to compile a right-to-left document with LuaLaTeX and a person or a text extraction to check direction and shaping in the PDF; the export of right-to-left text is not covered by any test in this lane, and ticking it from the web view would be a claim without a run.

**Notes:** Lane FE-105-117, round 3 (2026-11-13): nothing ticked. The web view now writes the document's base direction to the editing surface (`settings.dir` through `core.direction`, set in `packages/engine/src/view/web.ts` and re-applied when it changes), and a paragraph's own `dir` was already written (`packages/engine/src/schema.ts`). `packages/engine/test/fe-111-direction.test.ts` covers the paragraph part in Node. Criterion 1 needs a browser render, which was not run. Criterion 2 needs a browser run in each supported browser. Lane V-FE-097-112 (2026-11-13), not ticked: `packages/element/e2e/fe-111-visual-caret.spec.ts` measures where the collapsed caret is drawn after each ArrowRight, against a plain contenteditable reference of the same width, font, direction and text; it passes in Chromium and Firefox (`--project=chromium --project=firefox`, run with `--output` outside the shared test-results folder). In WebKit the selection of a shadow root is retargeted to the document (its anchor is the host's parent at offset 0), so the collapsed caret has an empty rect; the test is skipped there with that reason, and the criterion stays open in WebKit. Criterion 3 needs the FE-123 matrix extended to the corpus (FE-123's file is another story's; not edited). Criterion 4: the region clamp in `packages/engine/src/regions.ts` works on positions, not on the script, but the caret in right-to-left text was not run in a browser, so it stays unticked. Criterion 5 needs EXP, which is not built in this lane. Content support starts with left-to-right scripts; the web view inherits the browser's handling and LuaLaTeX handles the PDF (02 §11). The page view needs its own plan (FE-112).

### FE-112 Plan bidirectional, complex-script and East Asian layout for the page view

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| spike | cond | M | could | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 02 §11, §9.3 · **Requirements:** R1 · **Depends on:** SPK-013, FE-076, FE-080

As a maintainer, I want a written plan for bidirectional text, complex-script shaping checks and East Asian line breaking in the page view, so that the engine's interfaces can carry them.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the page view goes ahead, when the spike ends, then a written plan covers bidirectional reordering, caret movement and selection rectangles in the layout tree.
- [x] Given complex scripts, when the plan is written, then it states how the measurement provider returns clusters and how shaping is checked against the typeset result.
- [x] Given East Asian text, when the plan is written, then it states which line-breaking rules and tailoring are needed beyond FE-080.
- [ ] Given the plan, when it is reviewed, then it names what each provider interface (FE-076) must carry and what the WASM epic must provide, and gives a size estimate.

**Evidence:** `docs/adr/0208-lane-FE-105-117.md`, section "Plan for FE-112" (criteria 1 to 3: sections 1 to 3; criterion 4: section 4, which names the interfaces, the WASM needs and a size class per item, but is not reviewed).

**Notes:** Only if the page view goes ahead (SPK-013); phase `cond` because it also depends on the right-to-left content work of phase 4. Lane FE-105-117, round 3 (2026-11-13): the plan is written ahead of SPK-013 and is proposed, not decided. Criteria 1 to 3 are ticked on the plan's content; criterion 4 stays unticked until a maintainer reviews the plan. The plan reads the current `measure.ts`, `lines.ts` and `caret.ts`, and nothing in them does bidi reordering or cluster measurement, so the plan's new interface members are not built. Lane w1 (2026-11-18): blocked; nothing new could be ticked. Criterion 4 is true of the plan's content (section 4 names the interfaces, the WASM needs and a size class per item) but its condition is "when it is reviewed", and that review is a maintainer's: the story's persona is the maintainer, and the owner's answers of 2026-11-15 and 2026-11-17 do not cover it (decision 26 settles the page view and decision 29 its page breaks, neither the plan). A review written by the agent that is asked to tick the box would not be one, so the criterion stays open and the plan was not changed. Needs from others: a maintainer's review of `docs/adr/0208-lane-FE-105-117.md`, section "Plan for FE-112", and a note of who reviewed it and when.

### FE-113 View documents on a mobile browser

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | should | Viewer |

**Spec:** 02 §11, §12; 11 §4 · **Requirements:** R12 · **Depends on:** FE-066, FE-065

As a reader on a phone, I want to read a document in the web view, so that I can use it away from a desk.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a phone-width viewport in a supported mobile browser, when a document opens, then text reflows with no horizontal scrolling, scrolls by touch and can be pinch-zoomed.
- [x] Given a touch device, when any `view` value is requested, then the web view is used.
- [ ] Given the viewing experience, when it is tested in iOS Safari and Chrome on Android (device or emulation as the matrix defines), then reading, scrolling, comments and read-only mode work.
- [x] Given touch controls, when their size is measured, then they meet the target-size criterion (FE-104).

**Evidence (criterion 4, lane V-FE-113-128, round 6, 2026-11-14):** `packages/element/e2e/fe-113-mobile-target-size.spec.ts`: on the embed page's default shell with the editor mounted, every visible pointer control in the shadow root (buttons, menu items, tabs, checkboxes, combo boxes, switches, options, inputs, selects and links, the same selector as FE-104) is at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels, on Playwright's "Pixel 7" profile (Chromium) and "iPhone 13" profile (WebKit): touch, mobile flag, emulated. Scope: the default shell only; side panes, dialogs and the context menu are not measured on a phone here (as FE-104 criterion 5 notes), and Firefox has no mobile profile. Run with `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=57077 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/fe-113-mobile-target-size.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=webkit --retries=0`: 2 passed. Emulation on the desktop machine, not a phone.

**Evidence (criterion 2 unticked by the reviewer, U4 group 2 round 2: the outcome test below passes without any touch decision being applied, because the element has one view and does not call `selectView`; criterion 2 stays open until the element applies the decision and a test fails without it):** lane U-FE-113-128, 2026-11-13, `packages/element/e2e/fe-113-mobile-view.spec.ts`, run with `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/fe-113-mobile-view.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=webkit --project=firefox` on demo port 56070, at 390 by 844 CSS pixels with touch and the mobile flag. Criterion 1: reflow passed in all three (a 120-character word and a wrapping line; the page and the surface do not scroll sideways). Touch scrolling (`Input.synthesizeScrollGesture`) and pinch-zoom (`Input.synthesizePinchGesture`, `visualViewport.scale` above 1.2) passed in Chromium only; WebKit has no such gesture input in Playwright. This is Chromium's and WebKit's mobile emulation on the desktop machine, not a phone or a device. Criterion 2 (lane U-FE-097-112, 2026-11-13): `packages/element/e2e/fe-113-touch-view-choice.spec.ts` mounts the element with `view` `print`, `read` and `web` on a touch emulation (390 by 844 CSS pixels, `hasTouch`, `isMobile`) and checks that the web view's editing surface is the only view shown: 3 passed in Chromium; Firefox and WebKit report no touch points in this emulation and are skipped. Only the outcome is checked: the element has one view, so the web view is shown for every value, and `selectView` (`packages/element/src/view-select.ts`) is still not called by the element (the Notes above).

**Evidence (criterion 2, re-ticked in the U4 group 2 round 2 fix, 2026-11-13):** the element now applies the decision: `#viewChoice` in `packages/element/src/doc-editor.ts` passes `view` and `(any-pointer: coarse)` to `selectView`, and `view="read"` mounts the read view (FE-072). `packages/element/e2e/fe-113-desktop-view-choice.spec.ts` is the control: on a desktop `view="read"` shows the read view and no editing surface. `packages/element/e2e/fe-113-touch-view-choice.spec.ts`: on a touch emulation `view` `read`, `print` and `web` each show only the web view's editing surface, and in the same emulation with the coarse-pointer query answered false `view="read"` shows the read view again, so the web view comes from the touch decision. Helper: `packages/element/e2e/fe-113-view-choice-helpers.ts`. Run with `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts e2e/fe-113-touch-view-choice.spec.ts e2e/fe-113-desktop-view-choice.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit --retries=0` on demo port 56372: 18 passed, none skipped. Touch emulation: Chromium and WebKit use Playwright's `hasTouch` and `isMobile` at 390 by 844 CSS pixels; Firefox, which has no mobile emulation in Playwright, uses its own preferences (`ui.primaryPointerCapabilities`, `ui.allPointerCapabilities`, `dom.maxtouchpoints.testing.value`). For `print` the outcome is the same on a desktop while the page view is not built, so `read` is the value that tells the decision apart. This is browser emulation on the desktop machine, not a phone.

**Notes:** Mobile browsers get viewing in phase 1 and light editing in phase 4 (02 §12). Whether the surface is forced read-only on mobile before FE-114 is not stated (Open questions). Lane FE-105-117, round 2 (2026-11-13), no criterion ticked: the touch decision exists (`packages/element/src/view-select.ts`, `selectView` gives the web view for a touch device whatever `view` asks; `packages/element/test/view-select.test.ts` covers it over random attribute values), but nothing in the element reads touch input or calls `selectView` yet (`doc-editor.ts` keeps `view` on the attribute, FE-065 Notes), so criterion 2 is not met in the product. Lane U-FE-113-128 ticked criterion 1 (see Evidence). Criteria 3 and 4 need iOS Safari and Chrome on Android (device or emulation, with reading, scrolling, comments and read-only mode exercised, and the touch target sizes measured against FE-104): not run here. Lane V-FE-113-128, 2026-11-13, criterion 3 stays open: `packages/element/e2e/fe-113-mobile-browsers.spec.ts` (Chromium with the Pixel 7 emulation profile, WebKit with the iPhone 13 profile, both run in this lane: reading and read-only `mode="view"` passed, 4 passed) covers reading and read-only only. Scrolling was not proven: the embed document is shorter than the phone viewport, and mouse wheel is not available in mobile WebKit. Comments: the embed page's shell offers no `view.comments` entry (not in the header nor in the More menu), so the comments pane cannot be opened there; a page with the comments feature enabled is needed (needsFromOthers). Criterion 2 is ticked on the outcome only (lane U-FE-097-112, see Evidence): `selectView` is still not called by the element, and `view` has no behaviour yet (PRO-079), so the touch decision is not yet the reason the web view is shown. U4 group 2 round 2 fix: `doc-editor.ts` now calls `selectView` (wired with the read view of FE-072 by another lane, not by this fix), and criterion 2 is ticked on a test that fails without the decision (see the second Evidence line). The element reads touch input as `(any-pointer: coarse)`; whether a device with both a mouse and a touch screen counts as a touch device is not stated in 02 §11 (left as the element has it). Lane w1, 2026-11-17, criterion 3 stays open, nothing new ticked: a probe of the embed page on the Pixel 7 profile in Chromium (a throwaway spec, removed again) read the features the mounted editor has, `fields`, `presence`, `regions` and `suggest-enforced`. There is no `comments` feature, so the shell registers no `view.comments` command and no comments pane can be opened on the embed page, which is the state the earlier round recorded. Starting a thread is UX-028 (phase 2, not built), so a comments pane that had a feature flag and no threads would not show that comments work, and no stand-in was built. The second gap is scrolling in WebKit: Playwright has no touch scroll gesture there (see the Evidence for `fe-113-mobile-view.spec.ts`), so scrolling is proved in Chromium only, and only an iPhone, or WebKit with gesture input, can close it. The criterion is blocked on those two, not on work in this story.

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-18, criterion 3, comments half):** packages/element/e2e/fe-113-mobile-browsers.spec.ts, new test 'comments: a thread by another user is listed, opened and answered by touch' (Chromium with the Pixel 7 profile and WebKit with the iPhone 13 profile; the file's 3 tests passed in each, 6 runs, `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=56731 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/fe-113-mobile-browsers.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=webkit --retries=0`, 2026-11-18, emulation on a desktop machine, not a phone). Another user starts a thread on the first block through the mock backend's comments port; on the phone profile the element's comments pane lists it without a reload ('bob, Open, 0 replies'), the thread is opened with a tap, shows its text, takes a reply typed in the field and posted with a tap, and shows the reply; the page does not scroll sideways with the pane open. Reading and read-only mode were already run on both profiles (the other two tests of the file).

**Notes (lane w3, 2026-11-18, G71 wiring):** Criterion 3 stays unticked, now for one reason: scrolling by touch in iOS Safari. The comments half is closed by the test above (the element has the shell's thread service and the mock backend announces `comments`, so the shell offers `view.comments` and the pane works on both profiles). Touch scrolling is proved in Chromium only (packages/element/e2e/fe-113-mobile-view.spec.ts, `Input.synthesizeScrollGesture`); Playwright's WebKit has no touch scroll gesture or mouse wheel on the mobile profile, and scrolling it with `scrollTo` would test the engine's scroller, not touch. The criterion is met when an iPhone, or a WebKit run with gesture input, scrolls the page. Earlier: **Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-18):** Criterion 3 stays open; nothing new could be ticked. Checked against the decisions file and the code of this branch: docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md has no decision for FE-113, and nothing in it changes the matrix. The comments half is still blocked, though one reason is gone: the engine now lists the service's threads and draws their ranges (FE-048, this branch: `reportThread`, `ThreadSync`, the session handing `backend.comments` to the core). What is missing is the shell's side: no code in `packages/element/src` or `apps` provides the shell's `ThreadsService` (`packages/ui/src/core-api.ts`), so the shell offers no comments pane and registers no `view.comments` entry that works (the command is gated on the `comments` feature and the capability), and the mock backend has no thread port, so the embed page announces no `comments` feature. Building a `ThreadsService` for the element is UX-028 and UX-030 (phase 2), not this story, and a stand-in pane would not show that comments work on a phone. Scrolling in iOS Safari is the second gap: Playwright's WebKit has no touch scroll gesture or mouse wheel on the mobile profile, so touch scrolling is proved in Chromium (Pixel 7 profile) only; scrolling WebKit with `scrollTo` would test the engine's scroller, not touch. The criterion needs a thread service in the element and either an iPhone or a WebKit run with gesture input. Criteria 1, 2 and 4 are unchanged.

### FE-114 Edit lightly on a mobile browser

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 4 | L | should | Author |

**Spec:** 02 §11, §12; 11 §7 · **Requirements:** R12 · **Depends on:** FE-113, FE-115, FE-123

As an author on a phone or tablet, I want to make light edits in the web view, so that I can fix a sentence anywhere.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a mobile browser and the on-screen keyboard, when the caret is in the document, then the visual viewport API keeps the caret above the keyboard.
- [ ] Given typing, selection by the browser's own handles, and basic formatting, when they are used, then the document updates and syncs as on the desktop.
- [ ] Given the iOS and Android keyboards of the input matrix, when it runs on mobile in the web view, then it passes.
- [ ] Given a touch selection that starts inside a region, when it is extended, then it is clamped (FE-038).
- [ ] Given the guard, suggestion mode and the journal, when the user edits on mobile, then they behave as on the desktop, including when the page is hidden (FE-011).

**Evidence (lane w1, 2026-11-18, criterion 1):** `packages/element/src/keyboard-inset.ts` (the rules: `coveredHeight`, `scrollToCaret`, `watchKeyboardInset`) and `packages/element/src/keyboard-inset-dom.ts` (`attachKeyboardInset`, called by `mountEditor` in `packages/element/src/editor.ts`, stopped in `destroy()`): on a touch device (`(any-pointer: coarse)`) a `resize` of `window.visualViewport`, a change of the selection, or focus entering the document reads the part of the layout viewport the visual viewport leaves out at the bottom, sets it as `--de-keyboard-inset` on the host (the stylesheet adds it below `.de-body-frame`, `packages/ui/src/styles/shell.css`, so the page can scroll far enough), and scrolls the page or the nearest scrolling ancestor so that the caret of the focused view lies inside the visible part with an 8 px margin. Node.js: `packages/element/test/fe-114-keyboard-inset.test.ts` (16 tests, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node packages/element/test/fe-114-keyboard-inset.test.ts`, all pass; a fast-check property over 100 random carets and viewports checks the caret is inside the visible part after the scroll). Browser: `packages/element/e2e/fe-114-keyboard-inset.spec.ts`, Chromium, `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=4881 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/fe-114-keyboard-inset.spec.ts --project=chromium --retries=0`: 2 passed. At 390 by 844 CSS pixels with touch and the mobile flag, a real pinch gesture (`Input.synthesizePinchGesture` at the top of the screen) halves the visual viewport; the caret at the end of a 40-paragraph document, which was below the visible part, ends inside it, `--de-keyboard-inset` is set to more than 30 percent of the height, and a caret moved down by typing is followed. The control (the touch signal answered "no") leaves the caret outside the visible part and sets no inset, so the move comes from the element's code. Limit: Playwright has no on-screen keyboard, so the shrink of the visual viewport is made by pinch zoom, not by a keyboard; the geometry (a visual viewport that starts at the top and ends above the layout viewport's bottom) is the one an iOS keyboard gives. Chromium only (no pinch input in Firefox or WebKit under Playwright); no phone.

**Notes:** Lane w1 (2026-11-18): criterion 1 ticked (see Evidence). Criteria 2 to 5 stay unticked. Criterion 3 is a manual matrix on iOS and Android keyboards (FE-123's `input-method-matrix.ts` cases are manual) and depends on FE-115, which is blocked on an iOS device. Criterion 2 names selection by the browser's own handles: Playwright cannot start a touch selection that the browser honours (the FE-038 Notes record the probes), so the handles cannot be driven here; typing and formatting through a hardware-keyboard emulation would not show the soft keyboard's input events (composition, autocorrect), so no stand-in was written. Criterion 4 has the same limit as FE-038 criterion 2 (a touch selection); the clamp itself is input-agnostic (`regionClamp`, `packages/engine/src/regions.ts`). Criterion 5 would need the guard, suggestion mode and the page-hidden flush run through a touch input path, and the only path available here is the hardware keyboard on an emulated touch device, which is the desktop path again. Needs from others: a person with an iOS device and an Android device to run the FE-123 mobile cells, and a way to start a touch selection (device, or a Playwright release that supports it). Lane FE-105-117, round 3 (2026-11-13): not started; nothing ticked. No code under `packages/*/src` uses `window.visualViewport` (searched), so criterion 1 has no implementation to test. Criteria 2 to 5 depend on FE-113 (the touch decision in `packages/element/src/view-select.ts` is not yet wired into the element), FE-115 (the iOS input-method spike, which needs a device) and FE-123 (the matrix's mobile keyboard cases, which are manual). Criterion 4 (touch selection clamped inside a region) relies on FE-038's clamp, which is input-agnostic, but a touch selection needs a device to show it. Each criterion needs a phone-width browser run that this lane does not have.

### FE-115 Make input methods work on iOS Safari inside shadow roots

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| spike | 4 | M | should | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 02 §12 · **Requirements:** R12 · **Depends on:** FE-123, SPK-014

As a maintainer, I want the iOS Safari input-method problems inside shadow roots understood, so that mobile editing can be planned.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given the failures that S1 listed for an iOS keyboard, when the spike runs, then each is reproduced on a named device and iOS version.
- [ ] Given each failure, when the spike ends, then it has a workaround or a stated reason why there is none.
- [ ] Given the findings, when the spike ends, then a written recommendation says whether light editing on iOS is feasible in the web view, and what it costs.

**Notes:** Lane FE-105-117, round 3 (2026-11-13): not started; nothing ticked. Every criterion needs an iOS device and iOS version to reproduce the failures that S1 listed (SPK-014), and the lane has no device. The spike's input is S1's list, which is not in this package. The recommendation (criterion 3) follows the reproduction and so waits for it.

**Notes (lane w1, 2026-11-18):** Blocked; nothing new could be ticked. Criterion 1 asks for each failure to be reproduced on a named device and iOS version. This machine runs Windows and has no iPhone or iPad. Playwright's WebKit build on Windows (and its `iPhone` device descriptors, which only set a viewport, touch and user agent) is not iOS Safari: it has no iOS software keyboard or input-method editor, so a result from it would not reproduce an iOS failure and would not belong in the spike. The input of the spike is thinner than the criterion assumes: S1 (docs/spikes/2026-11-06-s1-prosemirror-shadow-root.md) lists the iOS 17 and current-iOS Safari pass as an unticked checklist item, with no recorded failures, so there is no list of failures to reproduce until a person runs that pass. The matrix case is `ios-keyboard-ko` in `packages/element/test/input-method-matrix.ts` (manual in every browser); neither can be run here. Criteria 2 and 3 follow criterion 1. Needs from others: a person with an iOS device and the project's test page, to run the iOS cell of the FE-123 matrix and report the iOS version.

## Browser support and performance (02 §12, §13)

### FE-116 Support the declared browser versions

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1b | M | must | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 02 §12, §3, §8; review F15 · **Requirements:** R12 · **Depends on:** FE-122, FND-002

As a maintainer, I want the supported browser versions to be a tested contract, so that the minimum versions are real.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the build configuration, when it is inspected, then it targets Chrome and Edge 137, Firefox 142 and Safari 17 as minimums.
- [ ] Given the CI matrix, when a change is tested, then the suite runs on those minimum versions and on the current stable versions.
- [x] Given the integration guide, when it is read, then it lists the minimum versions with their reason (`Selection.getComposedRanges()` for Chrome, Edge and Safari; the shadow-tree selection APIs for Firefox) and states that browsers below them are unsupported.
- [x] Given Safari, when the selection is read, then the editor uses `Selection.getComposedRanges()`; given Firefox, then the integration guide records the `ShadowRoot.prototype.getSelection` patch as a side effect (FE-066).

**Evidence:** `packages/element/scripts/vite-configs.ts` (`BROWSER_MINIMUMS` is `chrome137`, `edge137`, `firefox142`, `safari17`; every component build sets `build.target` to it in place of `es2022`) and `packages/element/test/browser-support.test.ts` (the four minimums, and the target of the library, viewer, demo and Svelte-host builds). The build was not run in this round. Criterion 3 (lane FE-105-117, round 6, 2026-11-13): `packages/element/docs/policies-and-side-effects.md` has a section `## Browser minimums` with one row per browser (versions and reasons taken from 02 §12) and the sentence that browsers below them are unsupported; `packages/element/test/fe-116-minimums.test.ts` checks the rows against `BROWSER_MINIMUMS` and the reasons. Run with `pnpm vitest run packages/element/test/fe-116-minimums.test.ts packages/element/test/browser-support.test.ts packages/element/test/integration-policies.test.ts`: 15 passed (the three files). Criterion 4 (lane U-FE-113-128, 2026-11-13): `packages/element/e2e/fe-116-composed-ranges.spec.ts` wraps `Selection.prototype.getComposedRanges` before the page runs and types in the S1 page; run with `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/fe-116-composed-ranges.spec.ts --project=webkit --project=chromium --project=firefox` on demo port 56070: 3 passed. WebKit made 32 calls; Chromium and Firefox made none (`test-results/evidence/fe-116-composed-ranges.<project>.json`). The Firefox `getSelection` patch is recorded in `packages/element/docs/policies-and-side-effects.md` (line 138).

**Notes:** The first draft attributed the Chrome floor wrongly (review F15). What the module does in a browser below the minimum is not specified (Open questions). Element-2 round 1 ticks criterion 1 only. Criterion 2 is CI configuration (root, not in this package). Criterion 3 needs the integration guide, which is not in this package. Criterion 4 needs the `Selection.getComposedRanges()` call and the `getSelection` patch in the editing code (FE-066), which is not built yet. Lane FE-105-117, round 2 (2026-11-13), no criterion ticked: `getComposedRanges` is called nowhere in `packages/element/src`, `packages/engine/src` or `packages/ui/src` (checked by search), and the only `getSelection` code is the shim in `packages/engine/src/view-support.ts`, which is for browsers without a shadow-root `getSelection`; no integration guide file exists under `docs/` (the name appears only in other documents). Lane FE-105-117, round 6: criterion 3 ticked (the guide is `packages/element/docs/policies-and-side-effects.md`, section `Browser minimums`, see Evidence). Criterion 4 is ticked (lane U-FE-113-128, see Evidence): prosemirror-view 1.42.9 (`dist/index.js`, the selection read) calls `selection.getComposedRanges` when it exists, which is the Safari path, as the WebKit run showed; the Firefox patch is recorded in the guide's global side effects. Criterion 2 is CI configuration (root). Lane w1, 2026-11-17, criterion 2 stays open, nothing new ticked: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs the browser jobs on the engines Playwright installs (Chromium, Edge, Firefox, WebKit), which are newer than the minimums, and its own comment says the minimum-version run is "to be added" (lines 82 to 85). The criterion needs a job that installs Chrome and Edge 137 and Firefox 142 (Safari 17 has no Linux or Windows build, so a macOS runner would be needed) and a hosted run to show it passing. `.github/` is root configuration that this lane does not edit, and no hosted run can be made from this machine, so the criterion is blocked on the integrator adding the job and on a run. Pinned versions of each browser to install are not given in the spec beyond the four minimums.

### FE-117 Publish a compatibility list for browser extensions

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | S | could | Integrating developer |

**Spec:** 02 §15 · **Requirements:** R12 · **Depends on:** FE-122

As an integrating developer, I want to know how grammar checkers and password managers behave with the editor, so that I can answer my users.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given a set of grammar-checking and password-manager browser extensions chosen by the maintainers, when the editor is tested with each in the supported browsers, then the observed behaviour (works, partly works, breaks) is recorded.
- [ ] Given the results, when they are published, then each entry names the extension, its version, the browser and the behaviour, with a workaround where one exists.
- [ ] Given a release, when it is cut, then the list is reviewed and the date of the last check is shown.
